Message from Chairman
In 2025, both Thai and global economies continued to face challenges stemming from a
slowdown in purchasing power, geopolitical volatility, and economic uncertainty across
various nations. Nevertheless, Thailand’s healthcare services sector maintained a vital role in
supporting the economy and enhancing people’s quality of life, driven by domestic healthcare
demands, an aging population, and Thailand’s strong potential as a global medical tourism
destination.

19
CoE
Center of Excellence
Limited Company Public SERVICES MEDICAL DUSIT BANGKOK
BDMS is committed to the continuous development
and elevation of patient care quality through inno-
vation and technology. The Company provide holistic
care that encompasses both physical and mental
well-being under a unified international medical
standard across the Bangkok Dusit Medical Services
Public Company Limited network. This excellence is
driven by 8 Center of Excellence (COEs) :
1. Trauma Center of Excellence
2. Orthopedic Center of Excellence
3. Cardiology Center of Excellence
4. Neuroscience Center of Excellence
5. Solid Cancer Center of Excellence
6. Hematology Cancer Center of Excellence
7. Dentistry Center of Excellence
8. Occupational Health Center of Excellence
BDMS collaborates on research and service system
development with leading domestic and international
universities. We drive our organization under the Value-
Based Healthcare concept, focusing on measurable
and comparable clinical outcomes. By utilizing large
data from our Centers of Excellence (COE) nationwide,
we enhance our service systems, ensuring safety and
quality through a single network-wide standard to build
patient confidence throughout the entire care journey.

20
Cardiology
Center of Excellence
“Precision, Speed, and Safety
for Enhanced Recovery”
The BDMS Heart and Vascular Center of Excellence serves as
the core for comprehensive care for complex cardiac condi-
tions. We cover everything from screening and diagnosis to
Evacuation using ECMO emergency treatment and rehabilitation under a unified
(Extracorporeal Membrane Oxygenation) network standard, supported by sub-specialized cardiologists
24/7.
Minimally Invasive Heart Surgery
• Heart Attack Care: Specialized treatment for acute myocardial infarction.
• Percutaneous Coronary Intervention (PCI): Boasts a 99% success rate
with a complication rate below 1%.
• Surgical Success: Approximately 97% success rate for heart valve and
aortic aneurysm surgeries.
• Efficiency: Reduced procedure times by 20–30% and decreased contrast
media usage by an average of 28%.
• Recovery: 96.35% of patients are able to return to their normal daily
activities within 3 months.
Neuroscience
Center of Excellence
“Fast, Precise, and Reduced
Recurrence for a Better Quality
of Life”
Neuroscience Center of Excellence provides rapid and accurate
emergency care by specialists available 24 hours a day,
covering diagnosis, treatment, and continuous rehabilitation.
Stroke Fast Track: An expedited management system:
• 24-hour Fast Track MRI with 100% of results read and diagnosed by
neuro-radiologists.
• Patients are cared for via a Stroke Pathway, ensuring efficient and
timely treatment.
• Outcomes: In-hospital mortality rate is below 3%, with zero readmissions
within 28 days.
• Advanced Treatment: Mechanical Thrombectomy for acute ischemic
stroke.
• Neuro ICU: Specialized critical care unit featuring Smart ICU systems
and real-time monitoring.
• AI Integration: Utilizing AI technology (researched with Heuron Co., Ltd.,
South Korea) to measure Hippocampal Volume. This analyzes brain
atrophy and Alzheimer’s disease from MRI scans, increasing diagnostic
precision and treatment planning.

21
Cancer
“360-Degree Holistic Care for
Personalized Treatment”
Center of Excellence
Cancer Center of Excellence focuses on complex cancer care,
emphasizing diagnostic accuracy, staging, and personalized
treatment plans determined by a Multidisciplinary Team (MDT).
Recovery (360๐) Prevention
TOTAL
360-Degree Treatment:
CANNCER
• Integrated workflow supporting collaborative decision-making among
CARE
various specialists.
• Systematic treatment protocols to reduce adverse events and compli-
cations. Treatment Diagnosis
• Precise treatments tailored to the stage of the disease and the patient's
physical condition.
• Cutting-Edge Technology: Preparing for the use of Advanced Proton
Therapy for highly precise cancer treatment.
Advanced Proton Technology
• Precision Medicine: Selecting the right treatment pathways to avoid
unnecessary procedures.
• Comprehensive Modalities: Including surgery, chemotherapy, targeted
therapy, hormone therapy, immunotherapy, nuclear medicine, stem
cell transplantation, interventional radiology, specialized radiotherapy,
integrative medicine, and palliative care.
• International Collaboration: Research partnership with ICHOM (Inter-
national Consortium for Health Outcomes Measurement) to evaluate
outcomes and quality of life for breast cancer patients, establishing
Best Practices and elevating care standards.
Orthopedic
Center of Excellence
“Precise, Rapid, and
Proven Recovery”
BDMS Orthopedic Center of Excellence focuses on treatments
requiring high procedural precision and rapid clinical decision-
making.
• Comprehensive Care: Covers everything from emergency assessment
and orthopedic surgery to mobility rehabilitation.
• Synergy: Combines advanced surgical technology with a synchronized
team of surgeons, anesthesiologists, emergency physicians, and reha-
bilitation specialists.
• VELYS™ Robotic-Assisted Surgery: Used for total knee replacement to
enhance alignment precision. Patients can often walk within 6 hours,
VELYS™ Advanced robotic-assisted technology for experience faster recovery, and maintain low infection and revision rates.
personalized total knee replacement
• Innovation: Implemented research on semi-supine patient positioning
for hip and femoral fractures, increasing surgical efficiency, especially
for the elderly and complex fracture cases.
Limited Company Public SERVICES MEDICAL DUSIT BANGKOK

22
Trauma
Center of Excellence
“Fast and Reassuring”
BDMS land, sea, and air patient transport systems have been
certified by CAMTS and CAMTS Global (USA) for two consecutive
years. We are ranked 2nd in Asia and 3rd in the world, while
our SURFACE: BOAT transport is the 1st in the world and first
in Asia to pass all categories of certification. This reflects
BDMS's capability in managing complex emergency cases
and specialized treatments.
• Regional Goal: To be a leading center in the Asia-Pacific region.
• End-to-End Care: From dispatch and on-site assistance to emergency
room stabilization and long-term rehabilitation, available 24/7.
• Expert Team: Staffed by emergency physicians and nurses specialized
in critical care transport.
• Network Power: Over 50 BDMS locations nationwide.
• Survival Rate: 99.6% survival rate for trauma inpatients (2023–2025).
• Innovation: Developed the Traction Splint for femoral fractures to reduce
pain and complications during transport.
• Trauma Activation Pathway: Combined with early-stage Whole Body
CT scans to reduce diagnostic time and improve triage accuracy, keeping
mortality rates below target.

23
Colorectal Care
Extended Center of Excellence
“Elevating Screening,
Reducing Colon Cancer Risks”
This Extended COE focuses on screening, early diag-
nosis, and prevention of colorectal cancer.
• High Detection Rate: Average Adenoma Detection Rate (ADR)
of 40.3%, significantly higher than the international standard
(≥25%).
• Quality Assurance: Focuses on standardized colonoscopy
procedures and bowel preparation protocols to reduce variability
and enhance prevention.
• Standardization: Develops group-wide clinical guidelines for
bowel preparation and endoscopic procedures.
• Clinical Excellence: Reduces variability, enhances diagnostic
quality, and strengthens colorectal cancer prevention capabilities.
Dental
Care
Extended Center of Excellence
“Precision Dentistry through
Digital Technology”
The Dental Care Center of Excellence serves as an Extended
Center of Expertise (Extended COE), dedicated to elevating
oral healthcare by enhancing precision, convenience, and
accessibility.
Technology and Outcomes:
• Utilization of digital imaging technology and CAD/CAM systems to
enhance accuracy in design and fabrication
• Reduction in treatment steps and overall treatment time
• Improved consistency of clinical outcomes
• Faster treatment for patients with fewer required visits

24
Occupational
Health –
Extended
Center of Excellence
“Proactive Prevention for
Sustainable Workforce Health”
This Extended COE focuses on systemic workforce health
management, covering risk assessment, health surveillance,
and workplace health promotion.
• Accurately identify risk factors and design proactive preventive measures
tailored to specific job functions and workforce demographics.
• Reduce the risk of work-related illnesses while enhancing the overall
efficiency of workforce healthcare.
• Support business continuity and sustainable operations within the
industrial sector.
• Reflect the role of Occupational Health as an Extended COE that
delivers value at both the organizational and population levels.

1
Structure
and Business
Operations
1.1 Policies and Business Overview
Bangkok Dusit Medical Services Public Company Limited
("the Company") was established on October 30, 1969,
under the name "Bangkok Dusit Medical Services Company
Limited" with an initial registered capital of 10 million baht.
The Company began operations with the opening of "Bangkok
Hospital" on February 26, 1972, and listed its shares on
the Stock Exchange of Thailand on October 2, 1991.
Subsequently, it registered as a public limited company
in 1994. As of now, the Company has a registered capital
totaling 1,758.22 million baht, of which 1,589.20 million
baht has been issued and paid up.
Currently, the Company is a major private hospital
business operator in the country, with a network of hospitals
in Thailand and Cambodia totaling 60 hospitals as of
December 31, 2025. These hospitals operate under six
brands, namely, Bangkok Hospital Group, Samitivej Hospital
Group, BNH Hospital, Phyathai Group, Paolo Hospital Group,
and Royal Hospital Group. Additionally, the Company is
involved in businesses related to healthcare prevention,
rehabilitation, and medical support services through its
subsidiaries such as BDMS Wellness Clinic Company Limited
and BDMS Wellness Resort Company Limited, as well as
businesses supporting medical services, pharmaceutical
production, IV Fluids manufactoring, and pharmacy and
medical supply retail businesses, among others.

28
1.1.2 Major Changes and Developments
2025
• BDMS has been ranked No. 1 as the global sustainability leader in 2025 in Health
Care Providers & Services sector, being selected as a member of "Dow Jones
Sustainability Indices" (DJSI) for the third consecutive year in the DJSI World index
and for the fifth consecutive year in the DJSI Emerging Markets index, with the
No. 1 score in the Governance & Economic dimension and Social dimension in
Health Care Providers & Services sector, as assessed by S&P Global. The DJSI is
a benchmark used to evaluate the sustainability performance of leading global
companies, ensuring comprehensive management across all ESG (Environmental,
Social, and Governance) aspects. This demonstrates BDMS's ability to generate
sustainable returns for investors and create long-term value for stakeholders.
The DJSI is recognized by institutional investors worldwide and serves as proof
of BDMS's performance on par with leading global organizations.
• BDMS has received an AA rating in the MSCI ESG Ratings, an index that measures
a resilience to manage financially relevant ESG risks of listed companies worldwide,
conducted by MSCI ESG Research, a globally recognized agency based in the
United States.

29
Limited Company Public SERVICES MEDICAL DUSIT BANGKOK
• BDMS has been consistently selected as a constituent of the FTSE4Good Index
Series, a leading global index that assesses sustainability performance, compiled
by FTSE Russell of the United Kingdom. It serves as a tool for investors seeking
to invest in companies with strong sustainability practices.
• Wattanosoth Cancer Hospital, under the management of Bangkok Dusit Medical
Services Public Company Limited, has received ICHOM Accreditation Program
Level 2 for the care of patients with non-metastatic breast cancer. This
achievement marks the hospital as the first in the world to receive this highest-
level accreditation from the International Consortium for Health Outcomes
Measurement (ICHOM), a global organization that sets standards for patient-
centered outcomes measurement. The accreditation emphasizes the assessment
of outcomes that matter most to patients, including quality of life, physical
health, and mental well-being, in order to advance value-based health care.
• BDMS has developed the deployment of BURT (BDMS Utilization Review
Technology), an artificial intelligence–based system designed to manage and
review the appropriate use of medical resources throughout the entire continuum
of care, including diagnosis, medication utilization, and length of hospital stay.
This represents a significant milestone in driving the organization toward Value-
Based Healthcare. The innovation enhances service efficiency, ensures that
patients receive appropriate and necessary care, delivers optimal clinical
outcomes at cost-effective levels, reduces the workload of medical personnel,
and strengthens transparency in resource management. The system has been
recognized by several insurance companies, enabling BDMS to create sustainable
value for patients, the healthcare system, and all stakeholders.
• Samitivej Public Company Limited, a subsidiary of BDMS, has opened “Samitivej
International Children’s Hospital”, a 103-bed hospital located within the Samitivej
Srinakarin Hospital campus. The hospital is staffed with pediatricians across all
subspecialties, as well as nurses and specialists providing comprehensive care
for children from infancy through adolescence. It serves as a fully integrated
pediatric center supporting patients from Thailand and the Asia-Pacific region,
delivering care with advanced medical technologies and through collaborations
with leading international institutions and hospitals.
• Bangkok Hospital Chiang Mai has opened a new 90-bed building to accommodate
the growing demand from both Thai and international patients, increasing
the hospital’s total bed capacity to 282 beds. The new building also expands
outpatient service capacity, particularly for clinics, internal medicine, oncology,
and preventive health centers.
• Phayathai Sriracha Hospital Public Company Limited, a BDMS subsidiary, has
opened “Phayathai Bowin Hospital” in Chonburi Province. The 220-bed hospital
is the BDMS’s 59th hospital, with 59 beds available in the first phase. It provides
healthcare services to residents in the Eastern Economic Corridor (EEC), serving
social security patients, health insurance beneficiaries, and self-pay patients.

30
• Bangkok Hospital Khao Yai Company Limited, a BDMS subsidiary, has opened
“Bangkok Hospital Khao Yai” in Nakhon Ratchasima Province. The 71-bed
hospital is the BDMS’s 60th hospital, with 28 beds available in the first phase.
It focuses on orthopedics and trauma care and also functions as a referral
center, in collaboration with Bangkok Hospital Ratchasima and Bangkok Hospital
Pak Chong, to serve residents and tourists in the Khao Yai area and surrounding
locations.
2024
• BDMS has collaborated with Perceptra, a Thai startup, to develop an innovative
AI-powered system for chest disease diagnosis (Teleradiology: Solution for
Medical Diagnosis). This system can detect up to eight major lung abnormalities
with 96% diagnostic accuracy in chest diseases.
BDMS has supported research and pilot application of this technology within
the network hospitals, integrating AI with teleconsultation provided by radiologists
across the BDMS network. This has enabled doctors to reduce diagnosis time
from 15 minutes per image to just 1 minute per image, increasing diagnostic
accuracy by 20-30% and reducing physician workload by over 40%. The system
also facilitates disease screening services across hospitals nationwide.
• The Company has implemented the "BDMS Innovation Incubation Program,"
a medical innovation development initiative through collaboration between
innovation networks from the public sector, private sector, and academic
institutions with the objective to develop medical innovations and promotet
Thai startups in order to strengthen the country’s competitiveness. The Company
launched the initiative in partnership with 7 medical technology startup
companies, opening up space in 11 hospitals within the BDMS network to pilot
the use of Thai medical technology. In this regards, workshops were organized
to brainstorm and develop business plans to drive the application of technology
into innovations that generate economic benefits.
• Pyathai Sriracha Hospital Public Company Limited, a subsidiary of BDMS, has
opened Pyathai Sriracha 2 Hospital, a 113-bed hospital (72 beds in the first
phase) located in Sriracha district, Chonburi province. The hospital aims to meet
the growing demand for healthcare services in the Eastern Economic Corridor
(EEC) industrial area.
• Bangkok Phuket International Company Limited, a subsidiary of BDMS, has opened
the Phuket Cancer Center at Bangkok Siriroj Hospital, the first radiation therapy
center in Phuket and the Andaman region. This center provides services to all
healthcare rights holders, including National Health Security (Gold Card) holders,
government employees, and Social Security members. The goal is to enable
quick access to treatment for people in Phuket and the Andaman region, with
more effective treatment outcomes. It also supports Phuket's strategy as a Medical
and Wellness Destination, promoting the province as a health tourism hub.

31
Limited Company Public SERVICES MEDICAL DUSIT BANGKOK
• Paolo Hospital Phaholyothin, under the BDMS group, has been rebranded to
"Phyathai Hospital Phaholyothin" to accommodate and respond to the needs
of its service recipients and the evolving context of the surrounding area. The
hospital has integrated digital technology to enhance its service to be more
convenient and modern, coupled with the quality and standards of medical
care at the Phyathai level, under the slogan "Expert for Active Generation"
2023
• The Company commenced rendering a service of Bangkok Cancer Hospital
Rayong in February 2023, the 57th network hospital of holistic cancer treatment.
The Hospital provides treatment concerning radiology, chemotherapy, cancer
patients transfer center, palliative care, and cancer screening with advanced.
The Hospital provides treatment concerning radiology, chemotherapy, cancer
patients transfer center, palliative care, and cancer screening with advanced
technology to ensure the most efficient result while enabling patients living in
the eastern region to gain access to the treatment. Bangkok Cancer Hospital
Rayong collaborates with Bangkok Hospital Rayong and Wattanosoth Cancer
Hospital, BDMS network hospitals offering excellent services as comprehensive
cancer centers as well as cooperates with many leading hospitals in the country
to elevate medical competency and is a referral center for cancer patients in
the eastern region.
• The Company commenced rendering a service of Bangkok Hospital Pluakdaeng
in June 2023, the 58th network hospital with a total of 59 beds during the first
phase (from the actual structure of 180 beds), located in Amphoe Pluakdaeng,
Rayong which is the Eastern Economic Corridor (EEC). The Hospital provides
tertiary services; for example, internal medicine, pediatrics, surgery, emergency
medicine, occupational medicine, neurosurgery, radiology and orthopedics, etc.
Bangkok Hospital Pluakdaeng collaborates with Bangkok Hospital Rayong, one
of BDMS Center of Excellence, in elevating medical competency, and is a referral
center in the eastern region, in response to increasing demands for medical care
owing to a higher population in the EEC area.
• The Dental Lab of BDMS Wellness Clinic has been appointed as a Dental Lab
certified and quality controlled by VITA from Germany. It is the first in the Asia
Pacific region to be officially certified with VITA Certified Partner Seal.

medical equipment. The Hospital has provided services to both outpatients and inpatients 24 hours a day. The

2 Bangkok Heart Hospital
A 7-floor hospital building (including the basement) with 54 beds in total. The Hospital is directly operated by
Bangkok Dusit Medical Services PCL and is located in Soi Soonvijai, next to Bangkok Hospital. Bangkok Heart
Hospital is the first private hospital in Thailand established from the success of Bangkok Heart Center which
was previously the specialized medical center under the operation of Bangkok Hospital. Owing to an increasing
number of cardiac patients, coupled with technological advancement in treating cardiac patients, the Company's
management decided to expand the facilities for better treatment of these patients. Therefore, "Bangkok Heart
Hospital" was established and has been well-equipped with 3 major factors; namely, the expertise of medical
personnel in every field, state-of-the-art technology, a complete range of services, starting from the prevention
of cardiac disease, treatment and rehabilitation of cardiac patients, according to international standards to ensure
the customers' utmost satisfaction.
Bangkok Heart Hospital consists of a skillful cardiac medical team in every field such as cardiologists, specialized
cardiologists who treat congestive heart failure and prevent cardiovascular disease, cardiac radiologists, cardiac
rehabilitation physicians who have been trained abroad such as U.S.A., Australia, England, Belgium, Germany, including
nurses and medical personnel who are well-prepared to provide treatment and care to cardiac patients based
on international standards. The Hospital offers diagnosis, treatment, surgery, prevention, and rehabilitation of
cardiovascular disease as well as transfers cardiac patients by mobile CCU 24 hours a day, 7 days a week. Bangkok
Heart Hospital is comprised of:

With its world-class medical services, coupled with the outstanding experiences in providing treatment of
cardiovascular disease in the region, Bangkok Heart Hospital is well-equipped with a full range of state-of-the-art
and high-quality innovations and medical equipment as detailed below:
Hybrid Operating Room
combines a cardiac catheterization lab and cardiac surgery room in a case where
the patient has some complications owing to cardiac catheterization or when
the coil is being inserted in the aorta, the surgery can be done instantly without
moving the patient's bed or room to ensure the utmost accuracy and safety of
treatment for a patient with heart valve malfunction or the elderly with a high
risk of complicated cardiac disease.
Multidetector Computed Tomography 256-slice
with new technology equipped with Dual Energy (spectral) system. This machine
helps with a more accurate diagnosis of calcified plaque under the intima layer
in patients with coronary artery disease. Software dual energy helps in the
subtraction of calcium, enabling clearer visibility of coronary arteries. Besides, this
machine helps detect the area where myocardial ischemia takes place which
relates to the coronary artery. This is the state-of-the-art innovation whereby
MDCT has been employed in CAD diagnosis, allowing for one-time diagnosis.
Cardiac Magnetic Resonance (CMR)
An innovation in the diagnosis of cardiac and coronary artery diseases without
an x-ray, enabling the physician to detect any abnormalities of coronary arteries
and cardiac muscles, including any abnormalities of the structure and heart valve.
Wireless Electrocardiogram Monitoring
used for continuous heart rhythm recording from 24 hours up to 7 days, and wired
devices connected to a recorder (Holter Monitoring) for 24-hour monitoring, to
assess cardiac rhythm during daily activities; evaluate ECG abnormalities in patients
with palpitations, sensations of skipped heartbeats, and dizziness or syncope;
and to differentiate whether symptoms are caused by cardiac arrhythmias.
Convenient, no hospitalization required, and patients can continue normal daily
activities.

41
Limited Company Public SERVICES MEDICAL DUSIT BANGKOK
CARTO-3D and EnSite MAPPING System
Advanced technologies for the treatment of cardiac arrhythmias, used to create
three-dimensional cardiac maps that display electrical conduction pathways and
catheter locations, enabling more precise and effective catheter ablation.
PULSED-FIELD ABLATION (PFA)
A catheter-based ablation technique using high-frequency electrical pulses to
treat atrial fibrillation and other cardiac arrhythmias without heat or extreme cold.
This technology delivers specialized electrical waveforms to abnormal electrical
sources to selectively destroy arrhythmogenic cells, restore normal cardiac function,
reduce complications, and lower recurrence rates of atrial arrhythmias.
CARDIOVASCULAR IMPLANTABLE ELECTRONIC DEVICES
(CIEDs)
Implantable cardiac electronic devices such as pacemakers, used to treat abnormally
slow heart rates or unexplained transient cardiac arrest, helping regulate and
restore normal heart rhythm.
Off-Pump CABG
enables the surgeon to go ahead and bypass the blocked artery while the heart
is still beating without the use of a cardiopulmonary bypass machine.
MINIMALLY Invasive Coronary Artery Bypass Grafting
(CABG)
minimally performs without splitting the breastbone. The surgical incision is small,
only 4-7 centimeters and hidden on the side of the chest, resulting in less
blood loss, reduced risk of infection or wound dehiscence, less pain, and faster
postoperative recovery.
Minimally Invasive Mitral Valve Surgery or Totally
3D Endoscopic Valve Surgery
advances valve surgery by using a small-incision technique, without opening the
sternum. The incision is only 4-5 centimeters, helping to reduce pain and promote
faster recovery.
Endovascular Aneurysmal Repair (EVAR)/
Thoracic Endovascular Aortic Repair (TEVAR)
Treatment of aortic aneurysms using stent graft insertion through a catheter,
known as Endovascular Aneurysm Repair (EVAR) or Thoracic Endovascular Aortic
Repair (TEVAR), or open surgery with stent placement to treat aortic aneurysms
or dissections. The procedure is performed by a specialized surgical team using
advanced tools and equipment to minimize the risk of complications.

42
ECMO (Extra Corporal Membrane Oxygenator)
and TRANSPORT ECMO
are devices that function as an artificial heart and lung, helping to pump blood
in place of the heart and exchange gases in place of the lungs. They work by
removing deoxygenated blood from the body to be oxygenated by the ECMO
machine, converting it into oxygen-rich blood. These devices are used for critically
ill patients who cannot be treated with conventional methods. They also enhance
safety during the transport of critically ill patients who previously could not be
moved, with the support of a highly specialized team of doctors, nurses, and
critical care transport professionals.
ROTABLATION ATHERECTOMY
a procedure to treat coronary artery stenosis using a tiny diamond-tipped drill
passed through a catheter. This technique addresses complex coronary artery
disease (Complex PCI) by breaking down hardened calcium deposits that block
coronary arteries, without the need for surgery.
INTRAVASCULAR LITHOTRIPSY (IVL)
A catheter-based shockwave therapy for breaking down calcium deposits in the
coronary arteries. This new technique enhances the effectiveness of treating complex
coronary artery stenosis via percutaneous coronary intervention (Complex PCI)
without causing injury to surrounding tissues or the vascular endothelium. It
increases the success rate of balloon angioplasty or stent implantation in patients
with heavily calcified coronary artery lesions.
TAVI/TAVR
(Transcatheter Aortic Valve Implantation/Replacement)
a minimally invasive procedure to repair or replace the aortic valve via a catheter
without opening the chest. It reduces risks in elderly patients, offering less pain,
faster recovery, and fewer complications.
TRANSCATHETER MITRAL VALVE REPAIR WITH MITRAL
VALVE CLIP
a catheter-based procedure using the Mitral Clip to repair a leaky mitral valve.
It avoids the need for sternotomy, minimizes blood loss, reduces complications,
and allows rapid recovery. Patients experience reduced shortness of breath,
improved ability to lie flat, better cardiac function, and a return to normal activities.
CAROTID ENDARTERECTOMY (CEA)
a surgical procedure to remove fatty deposits clogging the carotid arteries, which
supply blood to the brain. It reduces the risk of stroke in patients with mini-strokes
or transient ischemic attacks (TIAs).

43
Limited Company Public SERVICES MEDICAL DUSIT BANGKOK
TRANSCATHETER ATRIAL SEPTAL DEFECT (ASD) CLOSURE
a minimally invasive technique to close large congenital atrial septal defects (ASD)
using a catheter and a special occluder device. Transcatheter Patent Foramen
Ovale Closure, a similar catheter-based technique to close small congenital
defects (PFO) with an occluder device. Both procedures avoid surgery, involve
small incisions at the groin, allow quick recovery, and have high success rates
with low complications. These treatments can also alleviate migraine with aura
and lower the risk of acute stroke in working-age adults.
Uniportal VATS video-assisted thoracic surgery
(Uniportal VATS)
Apart from the decreased number and size of wounds, Electromagnetic Navigation
Bronchoscopy (ENB) is also employed whereby GPS helps clearly and accurately
detect the lung tumor which is smaller than 1 centimeter in size. This, in turn,
enables the specialized physician to perform the invasive surgery at the exact
location with a shorter operation time.
24-Hour Intervention
For patients experiencing chest pain with potential myocardial ischemia, coronary
artery disease, or heart attack. This service also includes the diagnosis, coronary
artery angiography, and angioplasty, including stent insertion performed by an
expert medical team 24 hours a day.
Mobile CCU (Mobile Cardiac Care Unit)
An intensive care unit on wheels imported from Germany. This special-equipped
ambulance is outfitted with state-of-the-art monitoring systems, equipment, and
life-saving pharmaceuticals used in the hospital's intensive care units. The CCU
is staffed with the very best physicians and nurses ready to transfer patients and
provide medical treatment to severe or sudden cardiac patients before being
admitted to the hospital.

3 Wattanosoth Hospital
A 47-bed hospital located in Soi Soonvijai, on the same campus as Bangkok Hospital, is known for its first private
dedicated cancer care institution in Thailand. The Hospital aims to provide quality service, coupled with cutting-
edge technology for the diagnosis and treatment of cancer, including expert physicians and multidisciplinary
teams for comprehensive cancer care. The Hospital is comprised of the following centers and clinics:
Readiness of Advanced Medical Technology to Provide Cancer Patient Care
Cyclotron
The Cyclotron is capable of producing PET radiopharmaceuticals such as F-18 FDG
used for PET/CT Scan and first introduced in late 2005 for use at Wattanosoth
Hospital and other hospitals equipped with PET/CT Scan but do not have Cyclotron
such as Bumrungrad Hospital, Chulalongkorn Hospital, Siriraj Hospital, Rajavithi
Hospital, Ramathibodi Hospital, Chulabhorn Hospital and Faculty of Medicine,
Khon Kaen University etc. In 2010, Wattanosoth Cancer Hospital successfully
launched C-11 Acetate for the first time in Thailand to facilitate the diagnosis of
cancer at the organ where F-18 FDG can work to a limited extent; for example,
liver cancer and prostate cancer. In 2012, the Hospital conducted research to
produce a new radiopharmaceutical called C-11 Pib or Pittsburgh Compound B
which can be applied to patients with Alzheimer's disease, and in the following
year, the Hospital successfully introduced F-18 FDOPA to help with the diagnosis
of Parkinson's and Neuroendocrine Tumor. In addition, in 2019, Ga-68 PSMA was
first introduced to facilitate the diagnosis of prostate cancer.
PET/CT Flow Motion
Two PET/CT Flow Motion to detect cancer to determine the stage of cancer and
how cancer is responding to the treatment as well as detect recurrences at an
early stage whereby over 10,000 cancer cases have already received this service
and is considered the series provided to the largest number of customers.
Therefore, this team is deemed the most experienced team providing such a
diagnosis.

46
LINAC Model Varian EDGE
A linear accelerator customizes high-energy x-rays offering higher accuracy
treatment while being able to make adjustments to suit the tumor's size and
shape with the use of 3D-CRT to position the treatment location. LINAC yields
high-energy x-rays around the small area with more precision, similar to surgery
or radiosurgery. It is also equipped with respiratory monitoring systems which
reduce the time required for radiological therapy with high-energy x-rays as well
as reduce damage done to tissue and surrounding organs which ultimately
minimizes long-term side effects while being convenient and speedy in that
patients may spend only 15 minutes in the radiation room.
Vital Beam
an innovation of radiotherapy that combines intensity-modulated radiotherapy
and image-guided radiation therapy to precisely pinpoint the tumor with the least
impact on the surrounding tissues.

and creating the hospital's atmosphere as a healing atmosphere as well as investing
in cancer specialists to make the hospital well-equipped with a medical team,
nurses, and staff ready to provide services to the patients 24 hours a day, 7 days
a week.

4 Bangkok International Hospital
A 172-bed hospital located in Soi Soonvijai, at the same campus as Bangkok Hospital, with the "Integrated Care
Services" provided by an experienced multidisciplinary team specialized in neurology, orthopedic, and spine
disease. The Hospital is well-equipped with 5 operating rooms (including hybrid operating rooms), 1 intervention
suite, and the intensive care unit to become the leader of the "Smart Hospital" in the Asia-Pacific region while
being able to truly respond to the ever-changing needs of the digital era.
Bangkok International Hospital or BIH is operated under the concept of
"Every treatment is an art". The Hospital, where art meets science, has
elevated "People" or multidisciplinary personnel to become a key factor
that drives the organization towards growth and sustainability. The Hospital
has consistently emphasized specialized medical skills and the mindset that
mainly stresses "the utmost satisfaction of patients" to ensure effective
healthcare delivery and holistic patient management. Also, the healthcare

This one-stop care center has emphasized the combination of art with

enhance medical treatment and minimize potential side effects; for example,

ARTIS Pheno
Cutting-edge robotic imaging that provides high resolution and detailed 3D imaging
to drive minimally invasive orthopedic surgery with more precision, accuracy, and

3-dimension x-ray computer or O-arm Navigation System
with the latest series called "O2", introducing Stealth 8 and IntraOperative Neuro

clearer pictures, coupled with more accurate and efficient data processing to

Hybrid OR
An innovation of an operation room that combines imaging systems; for example,

Biplane Interventional Suite
An operation room for cardiovascular disease with the installation of an angiogram

3-Tesla MRI scan
A non-invasive tool that uses strong magnetic fields at 3.0 Tesla provides excellent

including tissues, bones, and body structure.
Robot-assisted gait training (RAGT)
A physical therapy assisted by a robot to improve mobility of patients suffering

(Velys Robotic-Assisted Solutions: VRAS)
utilizes advanced technology to assist orthopedic surgeons in performing knee
replacement surgeries. It ensures safety without exposing patients to radiation

minimizes incision size, promotes faster recovery, reduces pain, improves mobility,
and, most importantly, extends the lifespan of the knee replacement.

5 Chiva Transitional Care Hospital
This 52-bed hospital has provided medical and rehabilitative care necessary for patients after surgery who require
physical therapy; for example, knee joint surgery, hip surgery and patients with nervous system diseases such
as post-stroke patients, patients with paralysis or traumatic brain injury, severe trauma patients with the injury
of many body systems or elderly patients with congenital disease. The Hospital helps minimize potential
complications from pain while providing rehabilitation for body and mind with a holistic approach to ensure
physical and mental readiness before returning home, enabling these patients to resume their normal lifestyle
more speedily. Nonetheless, in a case of emergency, patients can get prompt treatment and can be transferred
promptly.
The Hospital has emphasized "Holistic Medical Care"
performed by a team of physicians, geriatric physicians, rehabilitation physicians,
physical therapists, occupational therapists, speech therapists, pharmacists,
nutritionists, and registered nurses who cooperatively plan consecutive patient
care while taking good care of these patients like their relative, organizing meeting
to closely monitor the treatment, employing cutting-edge technology and designing
recreational activities program to promote recovery, relaxation, including physical
and mental exercise program with the aim that the patients can pursue their
healthy lifestyles, regain their strength and independence as well as be able to
participate in social activities. Besides, our patient rooms are private, spacious,
clean, safe, and well-equipped with complete facilities appropriate for patients
with limited movement, intelligent switches have been provided in case of
emergency and the Hospital's landscape was perfectly designed by professional

Medical Collaborations
Faculty of Medicine Bangkok Dusit Medical Services Public Company Limited (BDMS) has signed a
Siriraj Hospital, Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) with the Faculty of Medicine Siriraj Hospital,
Mahidol University Mahidol University, to collaborate in the care of pregnant women and fetal
therapy. The partnership aims to enhance service efficiency, promote and support
collaboration, and facilitate the exchange of essential information for maternal
and fetal healthcare. It also focuses on personnel development to strengthen
knowledge and clinical competencies, establish referral and transfer systems for
pregnant women, provide academic consultation, and continuously conduct joint
research in the future. This collaboration seeks to deliver the best possible quality
of life and contribute to the sustainable development of Thailand’s healthcare
system.
Oregon Health BDMS has maintained a long-standing collaboration with Oregon Health & Science
& Science University University (OHSU), a partner within BDMS’s International Health Alliance network,
(OHSU) since 2014. The partnership is aimed at enhancing BDMS’s capabilities toward
becoming an international medical hub through the development of Centers of
Excellence to meet healthcare needs in the Asia-Pacific region.
The collaboration includes ongoing consultation and training by OHSU experts in
key medical specialties. In 2025, BDMS continued its cooperation in occupational
medicine, gastrointestinal oncology, hematopoietic stem cell transplantation,
advanced cardiac care, virtual training programs, and rehabilitation medicine, with
a particular focus on sports medicine and cancer rehabilitation.
Tokushukai Bangkok Hospital Headquarters has signed a collaboration agreement with
Medical Corporation Tokushukai Medical Corporation, the largest private hospital network in Japan, to
Japan advance medical services, research, and sustainable workforce development.
The cooperation covers knowledge exchange, physician training, joint research,
teleconferencing, and international seminars, with the objective of improving the
effectiveness of medical care and expanding access for patients in both Thailand
and Japan, as well as promoting efficient exchange of medical expertise and
resources.
Al Orf Hospital, Bangkok Hospital Headquarters has entered into a collaboration agreement
Kuwait with Al Orf Hospital, Kuwait, to elevate standards of patient care and expand
international medical cooperation. The partnership focuses on knowledge
exchange, patient referrals, and healthcare workforce development to ensure
that patients from Kuwait and the Middle East receive the highest quality of care
in accordance with international standards.

Clinic, Rochester Campus, Minnesota, USA, beginning in 2024 in the field of Heart
Failure and expanding in 2025 to Preventive Cardiology.
In addition, Bangkok Hospital Pattaya, in collaboration with Mayo Clinic, organized
the “1st International Conference for Cardiology 2025” to facilitate global exchange
of knowledge and best practices in cardiovascular care.
As a result of this collaboration, Bangkok Hospital Pattaya’s cardiac care quality
has gained recognition both domestically and internationally, with outstanding
clinical outcomes. The hospital became the first institution to receive ICHOM
Level 2 certification for Value-Based Health Care in Coronary Artery Disease from
the International Consortium for Health Outcomes Measurement (ICHOM), reflecting
genuine advancement in patient-centered care aligned with international standards.
Prasitphand Development Public Company Limited, a BDMS group company, has
signed an academic collaboration MOU with the Faculty of Engineering, Thammasat
University, for the development and research of medical devices. The partnership
supports education in science and technology and promotes innovation to enhance
the effectiveness and benefits of medical device development.

University MOU with Chulalongkorn University for research and innovation development.
The partnership supports knowledge creation, research, and development of
innovations and technologies, as well as the exchange of data and resources, to
strengthen research capabilities at both local and national levels and to build
robust research and innovation networks that contribute to sustainable economic,
social, and environmental development.

In addition, BDMS Wellness Clinic and Bangkok Rayong Cancer Hospital have
expanded cooperation with Neem Hospital & Modawi Platform to the Breast
Cancer Wellness Program, which integrates multidisciplinary medical care with
preventive medicine. The program covers post-surgery and post-chemotherapy
rehabilitation, hormonal care, physical strengthening, side-effect management,
inflammation control, and long-term quality-of-life enhancement, enabling patients
to return to daily life with confidence and improved functional capacity.
BDMS Wellness Clinic has signed an MOU with Dusit Thani College to collaborate
on the development of Wellness Hospitality personnel. The partnership focuses
on curriculum development and workforce training in healthcare services and
wellness tourism to support the growth of Thailand’s wellness tourism industry.
It includes short- and long-term service program design, co-branded curricula,
expert exchange, hands-on workshops, cooperative education at BDMS Wellness
Clinic, and research and innovation in healthcare services and customer experience.
This collaboration aims to develop a high-quality workforce to meet market
demand and strengthen Thailand’s position as a global leader in wellness tourism.

57
International Markets
Neighboring Countries Market
During the first half of 2025, the number of new patients
from neighboring countries traveling to Thailand for medical
treatment remained at a relatively low level, mainly due
to economic constraints. Subsequently, in September 2025,
unrest between Thailand and Cambodia occurred, resulting
in a decline in Cambodian patients traveling to Thailand for
medical services, except for patients who require continuous
treatment and therefore continued to seek care.
China Market
In the first quarter of 2025, the number of Chinese
tourists traveling to Thailand was relatively low, primarily
due to China’s domestic economic conditions as well as
negative news related to travel safety. During the second
and third quarters, the number of Chinese tourists traveling
to Thailand declined from normal levels, even during
China’s National Day holiday in October, as most tourists
chose to travel to Japan and Vietnam instead. However,
in the fourth quarter, tensions between China and Japan
led many Chinese tourists to cancel trips to Japan and
choose Thailand and Vietnam as alternative destinations.
From a marketing perspective, the Company’s marketing
teams in Beijing and Shanghai continued to conduct marketing
activities in China, including participation in major health
Limited Company Public SERVICES MEDICAL DUSIT BANGKOK
fairs across the country. These efforts enhanced awareness
and recognition of hospitals within the BDMS network.
Arab Market
During the first to third quarters of 2025, marketing
teams from all BDMS hospitals serving Arab patients jointly
carried out marketing activities focused on providing up-
to-date information, listening to feedback and suggestions
for service improvement, and promoting new products and
services in both Thailand and Arab markets. These activities
covered patients, agents, and government agencies in
countries within the GCC region. As a result, following the
Ramadan period in the second quarter, the number of
patients from the Arab market receiving treatment at BDMS
hospitals increased continuously through the fourth quarter.
Value-based Healthcare
The Company is the first organization in Thailand to
adopt the Value-based Healthcare concept and is among
leading organizations worldwide in this area. The Company
emphasizes creating meaningful “value” and “outcomes”
for patients, such as improved recovery, the ability to return
to daily life with good quality, and effective management
of chronic diseases, relative to the budget and resources
used in treatment.
The Company is a key member of the International
Consortium for Health Outcomes Measurement (ICHOM),
a global non-profit organization dedicated to promoting
patient-centered care through the development and
implementation of patient-centered outcome measures
(ICHOM Sets), encompassing both clinical outcomes and
patients’ quality of life. To date, ICHOM has developed
46 standard outcome sets and continues to expand its
portfolio, with adoption by more than 500 healthcare
institutions in over 60 countries worldwide.
BDMS has presented its patient-centered care
achievements and co-developed patient-centered outcome
measurement standards for ICHOM’s global implementation.
Bangkok Hospital Pattaya was the first hospital within the
Group to receive ICHOM recognition for Coronary Artery
Disease and Stroke, and subsequently obtained additional
recognition for two other major disease groups: Heart Failure
and Diabetes, which require close and continuous care.

(3) Product and Service Procurement
Supply Chain Management
The Company places strong emphasis on developing a fully integrated supply
chain management system under the principle of “BDMS Seamless,” covering
demand planning, procurement, distribution, warehouse management, and actual
utilization in patient care processes - before admission, during treatment, and
throughout post-discharge follow-up. This integrated approach enhances operational
agility, reduces process duplication, increases transparency and traceability, and
mitigates the risk of product shortages, particularly for critical medical supplies.
It also supports effective cost management while maintaining high standards of
medical service quality.
Development of Procurement Systems and Data Infrastructure
The Company has standardized procurement processes across the organization
by developing an integrated procurement management system that connects all
key stages, including purchase requisitions, approvals, purchase order issuance,
product delivery, and goods receipt. This enables accurate and transparent
operations while reducing errors associated with paper-based documentation.
In addition, a centralized vendor database has been established to support
supplier qualification, selection, performance monitoring, and systematic supplier
risk assessment. This initiative helps mitigate risks arising from incomplete
information and enhances the effectiveness of governance and oversight.
Data-Driven Planning and Integrated Inventory Management
The Company analyzes actual hospital consumption data, inventory turnover
information, and historical purchasing records to identify trends and support
prudent sourcing and procurement planning. This ensures an appropriate balance
between product availability and optimal inventory levels.
A network-based inventory management approach is applied to enable end-
to-end visibility across the system and efficient distribution to hospitals within the
network. This reduces the risk of shortages and losses while maximizing resource
utilization, particularly for pharmaceuticals, medical supplies, and medical
equipment that require careful investment planning.

60
Supplier Risk Management and End-to-End Supply Chain Risk
The Company conducts systematic supplier risk assessments across multiple
dimensions, including product quality, delivery reliability, dependency on single-
source suppliers, external conditions, and environmental and sustainability risks-
especially for critical product categories. Enhanced monitoring measures and
contingency plans are in place to mitigate potential shortages and minimize impacts
on medical services within the hospital network.
The Company also monitors risks arising from global developments and
restrictions on specific raw materials that may affect sourcing and proactively
implements mitigation measures, such as supplier diversification and safety stock
planning.
Synergy Collaboration and Sustainable Procurement
The Company promotes collaboration both within the hospital network and
with external partners under the concept of “Supply Chain Synergy,” integrating
upstream and downstream processes - covering procurement planning, inventory
management, and logistics - into a unified system. This enhances operational
efficiency, shortens lead times, and strengthens hospitals’ readiness to provide
patient care at every stage.
Furthermore, the Company adheres to the principles of the Dow Jones
Sustainability Indices (DJSI) as a key guideline for developing sustainable procurement
(Green Procurement). Environmental, Social, and Governance (ESG) considerations
are integrated into supplier selection, evaluation, and monitoring processes. The
Company encourages the use of environmentally friendly products and supports
suppliers in improving their sustainability standards in alignment with the Company.
The Company continuously utilizes data analytics tools such as Business
Intelligence Dashboards to monitor Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) and Key
Risk Indicators (KRIs) related to the supply chain in real time. This enables proactive
situation assessment, early risk detection, and precise corrective actions, resulting
in a transparent, resilient supply chain capable of consistently meeting hospital
requirements.
These practices reflect the Company’s commitment to developing a high-
performance, sustainable, and transparent supply chain that supports the long-
term quality of patient care across the BDMS network.

61
Limited Company Public SERVICES MEDICAL DUSIT BANGKOK
Medical Personnel
Medical personnel are the cornerstone of the healthcare service system and
a key driver of the Company’s strategic success. The Company therefore places
strong emphasis on systematic workforce planning and capability development
through training programs, scholarships, and continuous professional development.
This ensures that physicians, dentists, nurses, pharmacists, and other healthcare
professionals possess the appropriate knowledge, competencies, and professional
attitudes in accordance with professional standards and patient safety requirements.
The Company implements a structured recruitment and selection process
for physicians, dentists, nurses, and other medical professionals with specialized
expertise, sourcing from reputable educational institutions both domestically
and internationally. Candidates are reviewed by the Medical Executive Committee
and hospital management committees to ensure high-quality personnel who can
collaborate effectively, thereby supporting accurate diagnosis, effective treatment,
and high-quality patient consultation.
Recognizing the continuously increasing demand for healthcare services and
rapid advancements in medical knowledge and innovation, the Company encourages
ongoing professional development through collaborations with leading universities
and hospitals both in Thailand and abroad-particularly within Centers of Excellence
such as cardiology, neurology, oncology, and orthopedics. Development activities
include participation in academic conferences, training programs, site visits, and
knowledge exchange in both onsite and online formats. Internally, the BDMS MSO
Platform is utilized to facilitate learning and knowledge sharing among hospitals
within the network.

62
In 2025, the Company focused on physician and dentist development through
the implementation of mandatory individual Key Performance Indicators (KPIs),
supported by the BDMS MSO Platform, enabling continuous performance self-
assessment and professional improvement. The Company also adopted medical
technologies and digital innovations-such as telemedicine, robotic-assisted surgery,
and artificial intelligence-to support clinical care and enhance patient safety,
alongside promoting medical research and innovation. In 2025, research projects
from the medical division accounted for 73% of all research projects, with
continuous publication of academic works at national and international levels.
The Company emphasizes ethical governance and retention of medical
personnel by requiring strict adherence to professional codes of conduct and
organizational policies, while providing appropriate compensation structures and
clear career progression pathways. As a result, engagement survey results among
physicians and nurses have shown a continuous upward trend, in line with the
Company’s policies for developing high-quality physicians and nurses. This
contributes to improved treatment quality, patient confidence, and a strong
corporate reputation over the long term.
(4) Assets used in Business Operation
The Company and its subsidiaries have fixed assets used for their business operations such as land, buildings and building
improvements, medical tools and equipment, structural equipment, furniture, fixture and office equipment, vehicles, buildings
under construction, and medical tools and equipment under installation, investment property as well as assets under the
rental agreement. Details of fixed assets as of 31 December 2025 can be seen in Attachment 4.
As of 31 December 2025, the Company and subsidiaries reported net tangibles at Baht 2,764 million. Most of these assets
consist of computer programs used for business operation of the hospital and will be recorded as expenditures of the
income statement and can be calculated by using a straight-line method according to the period expected to gain economic
interest of computer program between 5-10 years.
1.3 Shareholding Structure of the Company Group
The Company is the business operator of large private hospital in Thailand with a total of 60 network hospitals in
Thailand and Cambodia (as of 31 December 2025) and operated the business under the name of 6 hospital groups; namely,
Bangkok Hospital Group, Samitivej Hospital Group, BNH Hospital, Phyathai Hospital Group, Paolo Hospital Group and Royal
Hospital Group. In addition, the Company has operated business concerning the prevention and health rehabilitation;
namely, BDMS Wellness Clinic Company Limited and BDMS Wellness Resort Company Limited, including business which
support medical operation, drug manufacturing, saline manufacturing business and pharmacies and medical supplies
business etc.

1.6 Dividend Payment Policy
Dividend Payment Policy of the Company
The Company has the policy on dividend payment no less than 50 percent of net profit of consolidated financial
statements, depending on business expansion and capital requirement of the Company in each year. Such policy must be
approved at the shareholders’ meeting, except in a case of interim dividend payment which can be approved by the Board
of Directors. In a case where the Board of Directors deems that the Company has good operating results and sufficient liquidity.

Dividend Payment Policy of the Company
In a case of subsidiaries which are not listed on the Stock Exchange of Thailand, the Company has set up the policy for
such subsidiaries to allocate profits as reserves prescribed by law as well as cash reserves as deemed necessary for business
operations. Subsequently, the subsidiaries may allocate the dividends to the shareholders.
For subsidiaries which are listed on the Stock Exchange of Thailand, the dividend payment policy shall be determined
by the Board of Directors of each Company as deemed appropriate and in line with the policy that each subsidiary had
announced to the investors.

Innovation to Deliver Supply Chain
Value to Patients and Society
The Healthcare Access Crisis and Challenges
in Thailand’s Health System : An Urgent Agenda
Over the past several decades, Thailand has developed its universal healthcare system to a level widely recognized
internationaly. In particular, the Universal Coverage Scheme has played a vital role in reducing healthcare expenses
for the population and expanding equitable access to medical services. Nevertheless, in practice, Thailand’s
healthcare system continues to face challenges in expanding access to care, both in terms of quality and equity.
Amid rapid global changes, the national healthcare system is being tested by Thailand’s transition into a fully aged
society, the emergence of new diseases, and the growing burden of non-communicable diseases exacerbated by
environmental factors and changing lifestyles. These factors have led to a continuous rise in demand for healthcare
services, while available resources—such as healthcare personnel, budgets, and infrastructure—remain limited.
Furthermore, current economic conditions pose additional pressure on the stability of healthcare financing systems
worldwide, including Thailand. As a result, private health insurance providers have adjusted coverage models, such
as introducing co-payment mechanisms, to curb unnecessary utilization. This reflects the shared responsibility of
all sectors to enhance the efficient and appropriate use of healthcare resources in order to maintain the long-term
balance and sustainability of the national healthcare system.

69
Mission Toward Value-Based Healthcare :
BDMS Innovation Strategy for Sustainability
Bangkok Dusit Medical Services Public Company Limited (BDMS) recognizes that sustainable healthcare extends beyond
providing medical treatment that enables patients to live healthy lives. It also encompasses the efficient, transparent, and
equitable management of resources, achieved by balancing the interests of all stakeholders within the healthcare ecosystem.
Guided by a vision of medical excellence and sustainable development, BDMS has shifted its paradigm from being solely
a healthcare provider to becoming a co-creator of value-driven innovation. BDMS focuses on developing medical innovations
inspired and driven by its internal workforce, grounded in the belief in the capabilities of its people across the network.
At the same time, the Company actively builds collaborative partnerships with public sector agencies and emerging
entrepreneurs across the healthcare value chain, in order to uphold excellence in medical and healthcare service standards.
BDMS has developed an intelligent medical resource management innovation known as “BURT: BDMS Utilization Review
Technology”, an artificial intelligence system designed to manage medical resources effectively. With the conviction that
impactful innovation must be created by those who truly understand real-world clinical contexts, BDMS is committed to
fostering and nurturing domestic innovation. The primary objectives are to enhance the efficiency and standards of healthcare
delivery, reduce the workload of medical professionals, and address imbalances in resource utilization within the healthcare
system.
BURT: BDMS Utilization Review
Technology
Recognizing its critical role in driving the national healthcare system forward, BDMS has adopted a corporate strategy
centered on Value-Based Healthcare, with the objective of delivering the best clinical outcomes at appropriate and
accessible costs.
Medical resource utilization management is a vital process that begins when patients are admitted to the hospital. It involves
ensuring that medical resources are used appropriately in alignment with treatment plans, with continuous review throughout
the hospitalization period, and extends to the discharge stage to reassess resource utilization and preserve the highest value
for patients.

BURT is an internally developed innovation that integrates multidisciplinary medical expertise, including clinical
practice criteria established by specialist physicians, data analysis conducted by utilization review nurses, and
technological support from digital teams that enable seamless system integration. The result is a Thai-developed
medical innovation tailored to the national healthcare context, functioning as an “intelligent assistant” for physicians
and specialized nurses in medical resource management—professions currently facing significant shortages.

BURT has received both national and international recognition, including the SET Awards of Honor: Best Innovative
Company from the Stock Exchange of Thailand, and the “Product Innovation of the Year 2024 award from the
global ITIJ Awards in Vienna, Austria—underscoring the global potential of Thai innovation.

71
Scaling Toward Sustainability :
Building a Seamless Healthcare Ecosystem
BDMS’s innovation journey extends beyond service enhancement to the broader creation of shared value for society.
The Company continues to expand BURT through multi-stakeholder collaborations, partnering with health insurance
providers to enable seamless claims assessment processes, enhance transparency, and deliver smoother, faster
patient experiences. BDMS also supports emerging entrepreneurs by co-creating and testing BURT technologies
within its hospital network to improve future health risk prediction accuracy.
BDMS envisions AI-driven medical resource management systems as key governance tools to elevate population
health outcomes. Through collaboration with public agencies overseeing national healthcare schemes, BDMS aims
to promote national health data standards and develop scalable models for public healthcare resource management.
Conclusion : Advancing the Future of Thai Healthcare
with Artificial Intelligence and Compassion
With a strong commitment to internal innovation and responsible business practices, BDMS stands ready to serve
as a vital force in driving Thailand toward becoming a leading global Medical Hub, where technology and humanity
are seamlessly integrated for the sustainable well-being of all stakeholders.
BDMS places great emphasis on cultivating an innovation-driven culture by collaborating with medical professionals,
providing platforms and support for idea development, and building networks with stakeholders across the public
sector, academia, business partners, and emerging entrepreneurs. These efforts aim to elevate service standards,
reduce inefficiencies, enhance effectiveness, and create a sustainable balance within the healthcare ecosystem.
Ultimately, BDMS remains committed to delivering value-based healthcare, ensuring that patients receive care that
is appropriate, necessary, and cost-effective, in alignment with the principles of sustainable medicine.

Limited Company Public SERVICES MEDICAL DUSIT BANGKOK
Human Rights Risk
Nature of Risk
As an organization committed to conducting business responsibly, BDMS
recognizes that its operations may negatively impact the human rights of
individuals or stakeholders, either intentionally or unintentionally. This can
occur at any stage of the value chain, from production processes, employment,
procurement, to project implementation in various areas. Human rights violation
risks may arise from unfair employment practices, workplace discrimination,
violations of labor rights or the rights of vulnerable groups, or projects impacting
communities or the environment. Based on due diligence across the business
value chain in 2025, key human rights risk issues identified include risks of verbal
harassment among employees, risks of work conditions causing employee stress,
and risks to patient/customer safety.

76
Personal Data Protection Risk
Nature of Risk
As a leading healthcare service provider, BDMS collects, uses, and
processes a large volume of personal data from service users, patients,
employees, partners, and related parties, especially health data
considered "sensitive data" under the Personal Data Protection Act B.E.
2562 (PDPA). Collecting, using, or disclosing personal data not in
compliance with legal requirements may lead to violations of data
subjects' privacy rights and cause Data Breach incidents. Additionally,
risks may arise from employee errors, unauthorized access, insecure
technology systems, or outsourcing data processing to external parties
with insufficient data protection standards.
Risk Impact
Collecting, using, or disclosing personal data not in
compliance with the PDPA, including personal data breach
incidents, may subject the Company to legal penalties
(criminal and administrative) and obligations to directly
compensate data subjects. It may also damage the
reputation and confidence of service users, patients,
investors, and the public in BDMS's operational standards.
Risk Management Measures
• Announce and enforce a Data Privacy Policy covering authorities, and report promptly to the Information Security
all BDMS network units. Management Policy Committee.
• Appoint a Data Protection Officer (DPO) to continuously • Regularly and continuously conduct data security
oversee, supervise, and provide guidance on PDPA system testing and employee training to assess preparedness
compliance. against data risks, enhance awareness of personal data
• Implement data Access Control systems and data protection, and educate employees at all levels on
security technologies, such as Encryption and Audit Trail ethical data usage.

Cybersecurity Risk from Evolving Cyber Threats
Nature of Risk
BDMS continuously adopts digital technologies to
enhance user experience and improve service efficiency
and operations, e.g., platforms like My B+, Health Up,
Well by Samitivej, and BeDee Application, or the use
of artificial intelligence and data analytics to enhance
service efficiency, such as AI systems for chest
disease screening and integrating medical data with
comprehensive healthcare service systems. While
such technological advancements increase service
convenience, they also introduce risks from new,
rapidly evolving, and complex forms of cyber-attacks,
such as Malware, Ransomware, or Phishing & Spoofing.
Additionally, reliance on cloud systems and external
service providers for storing and processing large
amounts of data increases the chance of unauthorized
data access without stringent security management.
This risk may directly impact BDMS's information
technology systems and the continuity of services at
network hospitals.
Climate Change Risk Leading to Increased
Frequency and Severity of Natural Disasters
Nature of Risk
Global warming and "Global Boiling" phenomena are
causing climate change to become increasingly severe
and frequent. This includes more intense storms, flash
floods in low-lying areas (especially Bangkok and high-risk
provinces), prolonged droughts causing water shortages
for operations and services, and rising sea levels
potentially affecting coastal healthcare facilities.
Additionally, haze and air pollution increase health risks
for employees, service users, and patients, along with
earthquake risks in some regions, necessitating hospital
preparedness regarding building structure systems and
emergency response plans. These physical risks are highly
uncertain and may directly impact BDMS's business
operations concerning assets, infrastructure, services, and
the safety of service users and employees.

Medical Service Fee Collection Risk
Nature of Risk
Providing medical treatment to patients prior to fee
collection presents a risk that the hospital may not
receive the due payment. Key risk characteristics include:
patients not paying after treatment (due to the ethical
principle of treating first), collection risks from various
customer groups, and risks from ineffective debt
management, leading to organizational financial issues
such as non-payment, defaults, or bad debts.

2.2.2 Risks to Securities Holders' Investment
2.2.2.1 Risk from Uncertainty of Expected Investment Returns
Nature of Risk
Investors expect returns from investment in the form of
capital gains from changes in securities prices and
dividends. However, the Company's share price may
fluctuate and change either upwards or downwards due
to external factors beyond the Company's control, such
as the overall economic climate, changes in
industry-related policies, regulations, or conditions,
unforeseen abnormal situations impacting the Company's
operations, e.g., the COVID-19 pandemic.
Furthermore, the Company may have limitations in
paying dividends as expected by investors, as it must
consider various factors such as operating cash flow,
investment plans for business expansion, and loan
repayment obligations.
Risk Impact
Such factors may cause the Company's share price to fall
below the purchase price or rise above the selling price for
investors, and may also result in annual dividends being lower
than expected. This could impact total investment returns
and investor confidence.
Limited Company Public SERVICES MEDICAL DUSIT BANGKOK
Risk Management Measures
The Company emphasizes effective corporate governance
and cash flow management to strengthen the ability to
generate stable long-term returns while operating
prudently under changing economic conditions.
Moreover, the Company has a dividend policy to pay not
less than 50% of the consolidated net profit, considered
alongside investment plans and the Company's financial
status, to provide a degree of assurance to investors and
help reduce uncertainty regarding the returns investors
will receive.

Sustainability
Policies and Goals for
Sustainable Management
BDMS is committed to fostering a culture of sustainability
across all organizational activities, guided by the United
Nations Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) and covering
the three key dimensions of Environmental (E), Social (S),
and Governance (G). The Company conducts its business
based on the principles of transparency, fairness, and good
corporate governance, together with comprehensive risk
management, to ensure that all operational processes deliver
safe, high-quality medical services and generate maximum
benefits for service users and patients. At the same time,
BDMS strives to create valuable product and service innovations,
while considering equitable access to healthcare services
that meet international standards. The Company places
strong emphasis on respect for human rights, the promotion
of equality, and non-discrimination in all circumstances.
BDMS aims to create value throughout the entire value
chain and continuously promotes environmental management
in all organizational activities.

87
BDMS has established a Sustainability Development Committee comprising representatives from each business group,
responsible for driving operations, formulating policies, targets, and directions for sustainability governance. Sustainability
issues are considered in the context of the healthcare services business through a “Double Materiality” process to assess
risks and opportunities and to prioritize issues for strategic and sustainable business planning. The Company has defined
short-, medium-, and long-term sustainability plans, with the short-term plan for 2025 and the medium-term plan covering
2025–2030 to support the SDGs, and the long-term plan covering 2031–2050. Details are disclosed regularly in the annual
Sustainability Report to support the Company’s sustainable business development.
BDMS reaffirms its role as a “Healthcare Leader” and its commitment to enhancing well-being through a sustainable
pathway by strengthening innovation, digital capabilities, and accessible services for all groups (Equitable Access), alongside
sustainable resource management such as energy and water conservation, circular economy practices, and sustainable
supply chain management under the “Supply Chain Synergy” strategy. This approach enhances efficiency, continuity, and
resilience across the BDMS supply chain, including ESG supplier assessments to build a strong and effective supply chain.
The Company also supports business partners and the use of domestically produced products, while strengthening the
capabilities and competencies of employees and partners in transitioning toward sustainable business operations.

4 4.1 Operation and Financial Analysis
Business Overview
Bangkok Dusit Medical Services Public Company Limited
(“The Company”) (stock symbol: BDMS) is the largest private
Management healthcare provider in Thailand. It operates a network of
60 hospitals under six hospital groups: Bangkok Hospital,
Discussion Samitivej Hospital, BNH Hospital, Phyathai Hospital, Paolo
Hospital, and Royal Hospital. In addition, the Company and
and Analysis subsidiaries also operate Wellness Clinic and supporting
businesses such as medical laboratories, production and
distribution of pharmaceutical products and medical
equipment, pharmacies and hotel.

Limited Company Public SERVICES MEDICAL DUSIT BANGKOK
93
The Company and its subsidiaries reported consolidated
operating income for the year ended 31 December 2025
(“2025”) were Baht 113,206 million, increased by 4% YoY.
The growth was attributable to an increase in revenue
from hospital operations of 4% YoY driven by higher price
intensity from complex cases. Thai patient revenues grew
3% YoY while international patient revenues grew 5% YoY.
In 2025, revenue from international patients was affected
by the Thailand-Cambodia border conflict, which began
in mid-2025 and is considered an uncontrollable external
factor. Excluding revenue from Cambodian patients, total
hospital revenue would have grown by approximately 5%
YoY, and international patient revenue would have grown
by approximately 11% YoY from 2024.
The Company and its subsidiaries reported net profit of Baht 15,848 million, slightly decreased by 1% YoY. Excluding special
items related to impact from flooding in Hat Yai and accounting adjustments, totaling Baht 339 million (net of income tax),
the Company and its subsidiaries recorded an adjusted net profit of Baht 16,187 million, an increase of 2% from 2024.
Major Developments in 2025
1. TRIS Rating affirms BDMS and debentures rating at “AA+”, with a “stable” rating outlook.
TRIS Rating affirms the Company rating and senior unsecured debentures rating at “AA+”, with a “stable” rating outlook.
The ratings reflect BDMS’s strong market position as the largest private healthcare provider in Thailand with its extensive
network of hospitals and strong franchise of hospital brands. The ratings also take into consideration the Company’s
sound operating performance and strong balance sheet.
2. Hospital network expansion
• Samitivej Srinakarin Hospital (Bangkok Province) opened a new ‘Samitivej International Children Hospital’ building,
comprising of 103 beds, in March 2025.
• Bangkok Hospital Chiangmai (Chiangmai Province) expanded its capacity with a new 90-bed facility, which opened in
May 2025.
• Phyathai Bowin Hospital (Chonburi Province) commenced operations in July 2025, opening its first phase with 59 beds
out of a total structural capacity of 220 beds.
• Bangkok Hospital Khao Yai (Nakhon Ratchasima Province) commenced operations in November 2025, launching its
first phase with 28 beds out of a total structural capacity of 71 beds.
3. Thai- Cambodia border dispute
The conflict situation along the Thai–Cambodia border, which has persisted since June 2025, has caused travel difficulties
for Cambodian patients seeking medical treatment in Thailand, resulting in a decline in revenue from Cambodia patients
from approximately 3% of hospital revenue in in 2024 to approximately 2% in 2025. The Company is aware of the impact
of this situation and has revised our marketing strategy, both domestically and internationally, to mitigate the effect.

94
4. Flooding in the southern region of Thailand
In November 2025, severe flooding occurred in the southern region of Thailand, causing Bangkok Hospital Hat Yai (193
beds; Songkla province) and the surrounding subsidiaries to temporarily suspend operations for approximately three weeks.
The incident damages were partially reimbursed by the insurance company in 2025, while the remaining reimbursements
were expected to be received within 2026.

• Revenues from hospital operations were Baht 107,514 million, an increase of Baht 3,938 million or increased by 4%

Outpatient and inpatient revenues grew by 5% and 2% YoY, respectively, driven by higher price intensity from complex
cases. In 2025, the number of available beds increased due to the opening of new hospitals, while the number of
inpatients declined from 2024 as a result of the economic slowdown, causing overall occupancy rate to decrease from
67% in 2024 to 64% in 2025.

Baht 1,031 million from the beginning balance of Baht 7,373 million. As a result, the cash and cash equivalents at the end
of the year was Baht 6,342 million. Details of cash flow by activities during the year are as follows: -
Net cash flows from operating activities were Baht 23,355 million, mainly from profit in 2025.
Net cash used in investing activities was Baht 10,608 million comprising mainly from purchase of property, premises and
equipment of Baht 10,416 million for hospital network renovation.
Net cash used in financing activities was Baht 13,725 million due mainly to dividend payment of Baht 11,917 million and
debentures redemption of Baht 2,000 million, netted with an increase in short-term loans from financial institutions of Baht
1,000 million.
