100
In 2025, ROA was 10.2% while ROE was 15.4%, a slight decrease from 2024.
The current ratio of 1.0x slightly decreased from 2024 due to a decrease in cash & cash equivalents together with an increase
in short-term loans from financial institutions and the current portion of debentures.
The average inventory period (excluding real estate development for sales) increased from 13.5 days in 2024 to 14.0 days
in 2025. The inventories were mostly medicines and medical supplies. The Company and its subsidiaries have an appropriate
inventory management plan with internal control. The Company and its subsidiaries perform physical inventory count at
least once a year and record allowance for the expired, slow-moving and deteriorated inventories to reduce its cost to
net realizable value.
The average collection period slightly decreased from 40.9 days in 2024 to 40.7 days in 2025. The Company and subsidiaries
have credit policies and assess credit limit based on customer creditworthiness. The average credit term for domestic payers
is approximately 30-60 days while international payers are approximately 30-90 days. The credit terms are reviewed annually
to monitor and control credit risk.
The average payable period slightly decreased from 40.3 days in 2024 to 40.0 days in 2025.
Cash Cycle slightly increased from 14.1 days in 2024 to 14.6 days in 2025.
For leverage ratios analysis, interest coverage ratio increased from 61.3x in 2024 to 75.8x in 2025 mainly from good
performance and a decrease in finance expenses in 2025. Debt service coverage ratio (DSCR) decreased from 5.5x in 2024
to 3.9x in 2025 due to higher current portion of interest-bearing debt.
Total interest-bearing debt to EBITDA slightly decreased to 0.6x in 2025 while net interest-bearing debt to EBITDA remained
unchanged at 0.4x in 2024 and 2025.
For debt-to-equity ratio analysis, total interest-bearing debt to equity remained unchanged at 0.2x. Net interest bearing
debt to equity also remained unchanged at 0.1x as of both 31 December 2024 and 31 December 2025.
The Company and its subsidiaries strongly believe that it has sufficient liquidity to repay its debt within the covenants and
will continue to be able to successfully adapt as the situation evolves.
Healthcare Industry Outlook
For 2026, the private hospital business in Thailand is expected to continue to expand, despite facing economic conditions
that may remain sluggish from the previous year. The Company has been adapting by developing new technologies and
enhancing its capabilities to address increasing competition and market challenges, while also improving cost efficiency
across operations. In the long term, the Company remains confident in the growth potential of Thailand’s private hospital
sector, supported by demand from both Thai and international patients, particularly due to the rising proportion of the
elderly population. Thailand continues to offer high-quality and cost-effective medical services and is also a leading destination
for tourism, helping to attract medical tourists sustainably in the long term.

Corporate
Governance
Policy
6.1 Overview of Corporate Governance
Policy and Guidelines
The Company recognizes its role, duty and responsibility
in practicing good corporate governance and believes that
corporate governance shall determine the structure and
relationship among shareholders, the Board of Directors,
the management and all stake holders with the aim to
increase the competitiveness and business value for the
shareholders in the long run as well as to consider the
benefits of stakeholders which in turn will ensure the
Company’s continuous and sustainable growth.
The Board of Directors has established good corporate
governance policy as guidelines for all directors, executives
and employees, and also appointed the Corporate
Governance Committee to supervise and screen matters
relating to corporate governance as well as monitor and
review the existing corporate governance policy to ensure
that it is up-to-date and consistent with current situation.
The Committee shall also ensure that the policy is in line
with good corporate governance principles determined by
the regulatory body as well as international standards.
The Committee shall propose such policy for the Board
of Directors’ review and approval at least once a year.
The Board has constantly emphasized good corporate
governance through integration of policies and business

6.1.2 Policies and Practices to Shareholders
and Stakeholders
Policies and Practices to Shareholders
The Company aims to maximize long-term benefits for
shareholders by managing the business to achieve sustainable
growth and profitability under good governance and social
responsibility while striving to maintain competitiveness
by carefully considering current and future business risks.
The Company emphasizes consistent profitability through
continuous long-term business development, effective
internal controls, audit systems, and robust risk management.
This approach serves as a way to reward and demonstrate
accountability to all shareholders. Furthermore, the Company respects the rights of the

2. Digital Transition Program
In an era of rapid technological change, digital trans-
formation is a critical process for developing a sustainable
and competitive organization. The use of digital systems
helps streamline operations, reduce duplication, minimize
errors, and lower long-term costs. The Company encourages

126
and supports employees in learning and strengthening their digital technology capabilities, developing skills in using digital
tools and working with AI to accommodate changes in the business environment and improve operational efficiency. These
initiatives are carried out under measures to mitigate risks related to data security, privacy, and the accuracy of outputs
to prevent potential harm to the organization and individuals. Examples of technology-related development and training
programs include Information Security Awareness, Building Digital Culture, Leading Change in Digital Transformation, Digital
Marketing and Sustainability, etc.
3. Cultural Education Program
The Company aims to cultivate an organizational culture aligned with its core values, emphasizing transparency and
accountability. Employees perform their duties professionally with empathy and understanding, and deliver customer
service with cultural and language awareness. The Company also promotes a culture of learning, collaboration, and openness
to diversity. Training programs include:
• “Anti-corruption in the Organization” to foster a transparent organizational culture, conduct business in accordance
with good corporate governance, eliminate conflicts of interest, and prevent misuse of inside information.
• “BDMS Risk Management” (Departmental Risk Assessment) to enhance understanding and develop skills among
executives and employees regarding enterprise risk management principles and practices, and to embed risk
management as part of the organizational culture.
• “BDMS Brain Treasury” (Knowledge Repository for Lifelong Learning), offering blended learning through classroom
learning, virtual classroom learning, and online self-learning via an e-learning system. Employees and medical personnel
can access learning content through mobile devices or computers. In this year, a total of 5,225 courses were available,
with more than 2,111,712 hours of online self-learning participation across the Company.
• “Happy Service” (Service with Heart), based on the belief that sustainable service excellence requires both customer
satisfaction and employee happiness. The program focuses on cultivating positive attitudes, mental well-being, and
proactive service skills to differentiate the Company, enhance its image, and elevate service standards.
• “Provident Fund & Investment Policy”, providing knowledge about provident funds and investment policies to support
employees’ long-term financial security.
• “Plan Your Happy Life”, preparing employees aged 50 and above for a stable and high-quality retirement through
retirement planning.

Policy on Customers
Patient Safety and Value-Based Healthcare: Driving Sustainable Healthcare Value
In an era where healthcare systems face challenges from chronic diseases, increasing complexity of treatments,
and rising patient expectations, BDMS is committed to achieving medical excellence and adopting a healthcare strategy
focused on “Value-Based Healthcare (VBC).” This approach emphasizes quality of life, patient safety, and patient
experience, alongside the efficient use of resources throughout the entire care continuum (value chain). The Company
integrates professional standards of care with medical technology innovations to deliver comprehensive care ranging
from disease screening and prevention, treatment, to long-term follow-up after patients return to their communities.
The Ecosystem of Care 360° Project applies the principles of Value-Based Care and Population Screening. It has
been developed to create a comprehensive healthcare ecosystem for corporate partner organizations, connecting all
stages of care-from disease prevention and population-level risk reduction (Population Health Management) to treatment
and continuous follow-up-thereby creating shared quality and value for patients, organizations, and communities.
The project is designed with a person-centered care approach and integrates collaboration among multidisciplinary
teams from Centers of Excellence, such as cardiology, neurology, gastrointestinal cancer, breast cancer, diabetes, mental
health, nutrition, and rehabilitation medicine. Operations cover the following key processes:
• TEST-Proactive disease risk screening and assessment via the BPH Connect application.
• TALK-Building understanding and promoting behavior change through preventive programs, including community
and organizational outreach, as well as 24/7 online consultation via the BPH Connect application and Line OA.

The results demonstrate that proactive screening and the use of risk assessment tools enable early detection of risk
conditions and significant diseases, allowing timely treatment, reducing severe complications, lowering long-term costs, and
improving patients’ quality of life-key objectives of Value-Based Care.
The project plans to expand its operations to hospitals in the Eastern region and other hospitals within the BDMS network
in order to broaden service coverage for corporate health check-up clients, encompassing a total network of 3,200 payors.
Patient Experience as a Key Success Factor in Healthcare
Patient experience is a critical factor contributing to the success of healthcare delivery. It is an essential element reflecting
the quality of care and aligns with the principles of Value-Based Care, which emphasize outcomes that matter to patients.
BDMS places strong importance on patient experience and has established a systematic process to measure it in accordance
with the international framework of the Agency for Healthcare Research and Quality (AHRQ).
BDMS conducts patient experience surveys covering key dimensions such as physician and nurse communication, clarity
of treatment explanations, responsiveness to concerns, medication safety, and discharge readiness.
The survey results reflect the genuine needs of patients, which BDMS uses to improve services, design experiences
aligned with patient expectations, enhance the effectiveness of clinical communication, strengthen the provision of information
and discharge instructions, and reinforce post-discharge support systems.
BDMS is committed to creating positive, safe, and trustworthy experiences to elevate the quality of care and achieve
sustainable health outcomes for all patients.

Service Recipient Experience
Customer satisfaction is the result of perceived quality, perceived value, and customer expectations, all of which combine
to create positive engagement that leads to customer loyalty and advocacy-where customers are willing to recommend
the services to others. These factors represent a vital driving force for the organization’s growth.
BDMS assesses customer loyalty and referral potential through the Net Promoter Score (NPS), which measures “customers’
willingness to recommend the hospital to friends or acquaintances.” This score reflects trust and positive engagement.
The organization can therefore use these results to develop and strengthen a loyal customer base, enhance brand strength,
and expand service utilization through word-of-mouth within families and communities, which are key factors for the
sustainability of both service quality and organizational performance.

they cause harm to patients. The Company conducts annual assessments of its safety culture to identify strengths and
areas for improvement, including communication patterns built on mutual trust and a shared recognition of the importance
of safety. Based on the 2025 staff survey, the following results were found:

Policy on Creditors
The Company has the policy to abide by contracts and commitments with the creditors of all types, whether in terms of
compliance with the contract, objectives of spending money and payment, maintenance of the equality of collateral (if any),
payment period as well as other conditions or matters agreed with the creditors. Consequently, the Company has determined
various policies to be employed as operational guidelines within the organization. These policies are indicated below:
• Changes in policies/ information/ management structure of the Company: The Company has the policy that the unit
directly relating to the creditor must prepare letter or record to notify the creditor in case of any changes in policy,
information or management structure of the Company which are related to its creditors and to enable these creditors
to completely acknowledge the Company’s information and news.

7.2 Information of the Board of Directors
7.2.1 Board Structure
As of 31 December 2025, the Company’s Board of Directors consisted of a total of 16 directors, comprising 7 executive
directors (accounting for 43.75% of the total board), 3 non-executive directors (accounting for 18.75% of the total board),
and 6 independent directors (accounting for 37.50% of the total board).
Definition
Executive Director
Executive directors are directors who are involved with full-time management and receive monthly compensation from
the Company in the form of salary or other compensation as identified in the Company’s regulations or benefit plan.
Non-Executive Director
Non-executive directors are directors who are not involved with full-time management of the Company nor do they
receive compensation in the form of salary. However, these non-executive directors may be appointed or act as
representatives of major shareholders.
Independent Director
Independent directors are directors who are not involved in the management of the Company, its subsidiaries, affiliates
or other related companies. They are also independent of the management, major shareholders or any other persons
with an authority to take over the business. These independent directors must not possess any business or have any
relations in terms of business interest with the Company, its subsidiaries, affiliates or other relevant companies which
may affect the interest of the Company and/or the shareholders.

The Audit Committee has been granted full authority by the Board of Directors to carry out its duties which include
systematic monitoring of business practices, ensuring efficient and strict compliance with internal control measures and legal
compliance regarding information disclosure and determining risk management relating to the Company’s business and
finance. At present, all 3 Audit Committee members are knowledgeable and possess direct experience in reviewing the
reliability of the Company’s financial statements.
In planning the internal audit, the Audit Committee shall assign the internal auditor to plan the audit of the Company
for the entire year and report the activities of the internal audit at the Board of Director’s meeting immediately after each
meeting.
At the Audit Committee meeting that deal with the financial statements and disclosure of financial reports of the
Company, the Chief Financial Officer and the Assistant Chief Financial Officer shall attend. Prior to meeting on the financial
statements with the management, the Audit Committee shall meet with the accounting auditors separately and without
the presence of the management, to inquire about important risks that may arise from the financial statements or financial
status or any limitations that have been found in the auditing process of the financial statements of the Company and its
subsidiaries. The Audit Committee also conducts an annual meeting with the auditors without the presence of the management.
This is to ensure that the auditors shall act independently without any restrictions in the auditing process.

Nomination and Remuneration Committee
In order to comply with good corporate governance of listed companies, the Board of Directors passed a resolution to
appoint the Nomination and Remuneration Committee which consists of 3 directors and two of the members are independent
directors. The Chairman of the Committee is the independent director and the Committee shall serve for a 3-year term
and has the duty to find qualified candidates for nomination as directors of the Company for both replacement of directors
who have retired as well as new director position and the President. Furthermore, the Committee shall determine the
guidelines on appropriate remuneration for the Board of Directors, Sub-Committee members and the President.

Executive Committee
To ensure that the Company has strictly adhered to the strategies, goals and plans set forth for the year, the Board of
Directors made the decision to appoint the Executive Committee. The Executive Committee is comprised of 6 members
in total. The Committee has the responsibility to supervise and ensure that the Company and its subsidiaries act in accor-
dance with the established plan. In addition, the Executive Committee shall screen matters from the management before
proposing to the Board of Directors as well as have the authority to approve normal business transactions or transactions
that support normal business activities of the Company within a given monetary limit. The approval authority of the Executive
Committee cannot be used with any persons that may have conflicts of interest, stakes or any other forms of conflict with
the Company or its subsidiaries. Such authority does not include connected transactions and purchase or sale of important
assets of the listed companies in accordance with the rules and regulations of the Stock Exchange of Thailand or other
related notifications. The Company must act in accordance with the rules and regulations of the Stock Exchange of Thailand
or other related notifications on such specific matter. Moreover, the approval authority of the Executive Committee does
not include matters which require approval from the shareholders’ meetings as stated in the Articles of the Company.
List of the Executive Committee Members as of 31 December 2025
1. Miss Poramaporn Prasarttong-Osoth, M.D. Chairperson
2. Mr. Chuladej Yossundharakul, M.D. Member
3. Mrs. Narumol Noi-am Member
4. Mr. Thongchai Jira-alongkorn Member
5. Mr. Chairat Panthuraamphorn, M.D. Member
6. Mr. Kongkiat Kespechara, M.D. Member
Scope of Duties and Responsibilities of the Executive Committee
1. Determine the vision, business direction, policies and strategies of the Company for further approval by the Board
of Directors.
2. Review the business plan, budget plan, annual expenditures, investment plans and Corporate Social Responsibility
(CSR) plan of the Company in accordance with the established policies and strategies and propose to the Board of
Directors for approval.
3. Supervise the Company’s operations to ensure their compliance with the established plan, including compliance
with related laws, regulations and guidelines of various agencies as well as the Articles of the Company.
4. Consider the report of the Company’s operations and propose to the Board of Directors for acknowledgement in
each quarter.
5. Approve investment and operation expenditures under the authority assigned by the Board of Directors.
6. Approve or review the credit line to subsidiaries under the authority assigned by the Board of Directors.
7. Approve investment or the disposal of investments budgets under the authority assigned by the Board of Directors.
8. Ensure that the Company has a suitable and concise internal audit system as well as risk management system and
control system which are in accordance with the suggestions and comments of the Audit Committee.
9. Screen and review the organizational structure and scope of duties and responsibilities of high-level personnel for
the Board of Directors’ approval.

150
10. Approve the succession plan for executive positions, from the Chief Officer or equivalent up to the Senior Executive
Vice President or equivalent.
11. Approve the appointment, transfer or termination of the management for the level up to the Senior Executive Vice
President or equivalent. In the case for the position of Senior Executive Vice President and the Chief Officer, the
Executive Committee shall report to the Board of Directors at the next meeting. The Executive Committee shall also
approve the candidates for nomination as representatives of the Company to the Board of Directors of subsidiaries
or a managing director so that the subsidiaries may nominate those candidates through the approval process as
stipulated by the laws or regulations and the Articles governing each company.
12. Screen and review the pay scale structure and principle of the Company’s and subsidiaries’ annual salary adjust-
ment for further approval (this does not include the position of the President) of the Board of Directors.
13. Appoint working teams to improve and solve various problems in accordance with the Company’s policies.
14. Review the Executive Committee’s charter annually and in a case where the Committee deems necessary to amend
the charter to suit the guidelines, regulations or circumstances that have changed, the Executive Committee shall
propose such changes to the Board of Directors for further approval.
15. Report the performance of the Executive Committee to the Board of Directors for acknowledgement.
16. Perform other duties as assigned by the Board of Directors.
Risk Management Committee
To ensure that the Company has strictly conformed to the established goals and objectives, the Board of Directors has
appointed the Risk Management Committee which is comprised of the directors and senior executives of the Company,
with at least 3 members being the Company’s director and each director shall be in the position for a 3-year term. Besides,
the Committee has the duty to screen overall risk management policy and guidelines of the Company for proposing to the
Board of Directors as well as adherence to the policy approved by the Board. In addition, the Committee shall review the
adequacy of risk management policy and system, including the efficiency of the system and the compliance with specified
policy.

Corporate Governance and Sustainability Committee
The Corporate Governance and Sustainability Committee has been established to support the Board of Directors in
formulating corporate governance policies and related practices, with the objective of continuously enhancing the Company’s
corporate governance standards. In addition, the Committee is responsible for defining the Company’s sustainability policies
covering environmental, social, and governance (Environmental, Social and Governance: ESG) aspects, with a view to creating
long-term value for the business.The Committee is comprised of 5 directors with an independent director as the Chairman
and each director shall serve for a 3-year term.

To ensure the efficiency and compliance of the Company’s operation regarding sustainability development according
to international standards and sustainability development policy, the Company has established BDMS Sustainability
Development Committee which is deemed a key mechanism in monitoring the organization’s sustainable development.
Such Committee consists of the President, as a Chairperson of the Committee, and executives from each business group
and reports to the Corporate Governance and Sustainability Committee.

7.6.1 Information of the Company Secretary, Head of Internal Audit and Person directly
assigned to be responsibile for Accounting
Company Secretary
To comply with the Securities and Exchange Act, the Board of Directors appointed Miss Kessara Wongsekate, Vice President,
as the Company Secretary to perform duties in accordance with relevant laws. The duties and responsibilities of the Company
Secretary are listed below:
1. Perform duties in line with the Securities and Exchange Act, the notifications of the Capital Market Supervisory Exchange
Act as well as ensure the disclosure and reporting of information under responsibility to the regulatory body in
accordance with the laws and regulations on the disclosure of the Company’s information.
2. Coordinate and monitor to ensure that the Company, Board of Directors and other relevant parties act in accordance
with the resolutions of the Board of Directors’ and the shareholders’ meetings as well as good corporate governance
principles.
3. Encourage the directors and executives to gain knowledge and attend training programs as required for the operations
of the directors and Sub-Committee members.
4. Provide necessary information to enable existing directors and newly appointed directors to carry out their duties.
5. Provide advice on regulations and guidelines for the directors and Sub-Committees’ acknowledgement.
6. Arrange for performance evaluation of the Board of Directors and the Sub-Committees.
7. Maintain important documents of the Company such as directors’ register, meeting invitations and minutes of the
Board of Directors’ meetings, shareholders’ meetings, annual report and reports on conflicts of interest of the directors
and the management.
8. Perform other duties as assigned.
Further details of the Company Secretary can be seen in Attachment 1.

Corporate

Report
8.1 Summary of Performance of Duties of
the Board of the Past Year
8.1.1 Nomination, Development and
Performance Evaluation of the Board of
Directors
In appointing directors, the Nomination and Remuneration
Committee, consisting of 2 independent directors out of a
total of 3 committee members, is responsible for recruiting
directors to replace the existing directors who resign by
rotation or proposing candidates for new appointment. The
Committee shall take into consideration the structure, size
and component of the Board by selecting suitable candidates
based on the qualifications and criteria set by the Committee
and submiting these names to the Board of Directors for
approval in the case of appointing a new director to fill a
vacancy before the end of the previous director's term. The
resolution to appoint the individual as a replacement must
receive a vote of no less than 3/4 of the remaining directors.
In the case of proposing a new director appointment, the
Nomination and Remuneration Committee will submit the
names to the Board of Directors to consider proposing such
candidate at the shareholders’ meeting for further approval.

1. Any one shareholder has the number of votes equal
to the number of shares owned.
2. Each shareholder shall use all his/her rights to vote
as stated in Clause 1 to elect one or more individuals as a
director, in the case of the election of more than one director,
the votes cannot be split to any one person.
3. The candidates shall be ranked in descending order
from the highest number of votes received to the lowest and
shall be appointed as directors in that order until all of the
director positions are filled. If the votes cast for the candidates
in descending order are tied which would otherwise cause
the number of directors to be exceeded, the Chairman of the
meeting shall cast the deciding vote.
Furthermore, the appointment and removal of a director
shall be as stated in the Company’s articles as follows:
1) The Board of Directors shall be comprised of at least
5 directors and no less than one half shall have a
residence in Thailand.
2) Directors shall be an individual with the following
qualifications:
• Be of a legal age
• Has not been filed for bankruptcy, is not incompetent
or near incompetent 4) Apart from resignation by rotation, director may be
• Has never been imprisoned or received sentence of removed in the following events:
imprisonment for wrongful acquisition of assets • Death
• Has never been fired or terminated from civil services • Voluntary resignation
or government agency for malfeasance • Unqualified or be legally ineligible
3) At each Annual General Meeting, at least one-third • Shareholders’ meeting passes a resolution for the
(1/3) of the directors shall resign by rotation. If the removal of a director with no less than three quarters
number of directors cannot be divided by three, then of the number of shares attending the meetings with
the closest number to one-third (1/3) of the directors the right to vote and with the total number of shares
shall resign. Directors who have resigned by rotation held by the shareholders attending the meeting with
may be re-elected as a director. the right to vote
• Court ordered resignation
5) Any directors who wish to resign may submit a letter
of resignation to the Company and such resignation
will be effective from the day the Company receives
such letter.
6) If a director position should become vacant due to
the reasons other than the resignation by rotation, the
Board of Directors shall select a qualified individual
who is not legally ineligible to the position at the
subsequent meeting of the Board of Directors, unless
the term of the vacant director position is less than
two months. In this case, the director who fills the
vacant position shall serve only until the end of the
remaining term. The Board of Directors’ vote for the
appointment of the director to fill the vacant position
shall be no less than three quarters of the remaining
directors.

164
7) In the case where there is a large number of vacant director positions and the required quorum cannot be met, the remaining
directors shall act on behalf of the Board of Directors only in organizing a shareholders’ meeting to elect individuals to fill all
of the vacant director positions. Such meeting shall be held within one month from the date that the number of vacant
director positions exceeds is less than the required quorum. Those individuals filling such vacant positions shall serve only
the remaining term of the director who previously held the position.
In the proposal to appoint directors at the shareholders' meeting in 2025, the Company provided an opportunity for minority
shareholders to nominate qualified individuals to the Board of Directors in advance in order that the Company may consider
nominating those persons at the shareholders’ meeting. However, none of the shareholders had nominated individuals for
consideration by the shareholders’ meeting. In addition, the Company had determined the method in voting whereby the
shareholders shall vote for directors individually in accordance with good corporate governance.
Knowledge Enhancement of Directors
The Company recognizes its roles, duties, and responsibilities towards the Company and therefore prioritizes the development of
knowledge for directors and executives to possess knowledge, abilities, skills, and potential by continuously supporting seminars
and training courses that are beneficial to their duties, both internally and externally. This is to promote directors' understanding
of business governance for maximum effectiveness and enable them to apply the knowledge to continuously implement good
corporate governance policies. All 16 directors and the Company Secretary have completed the Director Certification Program
and/or Director Accreditation Program. The Company has also supported directors in attending training on regulations, policies, or
requirements of the Stock Exchange of Thailand and the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), as well as completing important
courses beneficial to their duties organized by the Thai Institute of Directors (IOD), such as:
• Advanced Audit Committee Program (AACP)
• Finance Statements for Directors Program (FSD)
• Roles of Compensation Committee Program (RCC)
• Role of the Nomination and Governance Committee (RNGC)
• Anti-Corruption the Practical Guide (ACPG)
• Ethical Leadership Program (ELP)
In 2025, all 16 directors attended the seminar and training for knowledge enhancement as follows:

Nomination of Executives
The process of nominating the executives begins with the selection of good and
capable individuals with an attitude appropriate for the organization. The Company
has focused on new generation individuals with full potential, knowledge and
competence to develop and prepare for the opportunity to grow as well as possess
the potential to grow up to the top-level management through an evaluation by
Human Resources Department. In order to develop the individual’s skills and knowledge
of all aspects within the organization, the individual shall be assigned important tasks
and rotated among different departments to prepare him/her to fill any important
positions that may become vacant.
In the case of the top executive position of the organization (the President), the Board of Directors has assigned the Nomination and
Remuneration Committee to determine the method and criteria as well as qualifications of suitable candidates, and then present to
the Board of Directors for the appointment. Apart from the qualification in terms of knowledge and capabilities whereby the Nomination
and Remuneration Committee may consider the executive management within the organization or may bring in an external consultant
to select qualified candidates, the President shall be an ex officio member of the Board of Directors. Therefore, the candidate whom
the Nomination and Remuneration Committee believe to be well qualified and appropriate for the position of the President must
possess suitable qualifications and shall be appointed as a director on the Board by the shareholders’ meeting before assuming the
position of the President.
For the succession plan of top executive management, the Board of Directors assigns the Nomination and Remuneration Committee
to determine the procedures and criteria in selecting a successor as well as the suitable qualifications.

2. Other Remunerations
Medical Treatment Expenditures of External Directors
External directors who are not considered the Company’s employee/executive under contractual employment shall receive the
financial amounts of Baht 3 million per person per year for their medical treatment expenditures which can be employed only at Bangkok
Hospital and other network hospitals as resolved by the 2025 Annual General Meeting of Shareholders. Such financial amounts shall be
counted, starting from the date of the 2025 Annual General Meeting to the next meeting.
As of 10 April 2025 (the 2025 Annual General Meeting of Shareholders), there were 9 external directors who are not the Company’s
employees/executives under contractual employment attending the meeting; namely, 1. Professor Emeritus Santasiri Sornmani, M.D., 2.
Mr. Chavalit Sethameteekul, 3. Mr. Kan Trakulhoon, 4. Mr. Thongchai Jira-alongkorn, 5. Mr. Puttipong Prasarttong-Osoth, 6. Mr. Subhak
Siwaraksa, Ph.D., 7. Mr. Veerathai Santiprabhob, Ph.D., 8. Mr. Predee Daochai and 9. Clinical Professor Emeritus Piyasakol Sakolsatayadorn,
M.D.. From the date of the 2025 Annual General Meeting of Shareholders to the end of 2025, the Company reported medical treatment
expenditures for external directors at a total of Baht 1.45 million.
Directors and Sub-Committees
Apart from the remunerations in the form of meeting allowances and annual allowances, including medical treatment expenditures
of external directors which were approved by the shareholders’ meeting, the Company had no other remunerations for its directors
and Sub-Committees.

4. In reporting any stakes, the Company determines that the directors and the management shall report one’s stakes or that of a
related person to the Company Secretary as well as report any changes in such stake. The Company Secretary then has the duty to
store information and report any changes to the Chairman of the Board and the Board of Directors’ meeting in accordance with the
Securities and Exchange Act B.E. 2535 and its amendment. The Company shall use the information to control the transactions between
the Company and the directors or the management and/or persons related to the person mentioned earlier as required by law.
5. The Company determines the procedures and punishments when inside information for personal gain is discovered. This is in
accordance with the rules, regulations and Supervision of Using Inside Information Policy posted on the Company’s website.
During 2025, the Company communicated with directors, top executives, and persons with access to material inside information
directly through their email accounts on a quarterly basis, to ensure their awareness of and compliance with the Company’s policies
on the use of inside information and the prevention of conflicts of interest. In addition, the Company provided knowledge and training
on these matters, together with other corporate policies, to executives and employees through an e-learning system in which a total
of 33,603 executives and employees completed the training and assessment, representing 93% of the Company’s total workforce.
Through the year 2025, there were no reports on any misconduct relating to the misuse of inside information for personal gain,
unfair practices, or exploitation of investors in any way.

2) Policy on Anti-Corruption
The Company has the policy to conduct business with virtue and responsibility. The Board of Directors has approved the policy
on anti-corruption which has been communicated to personnel within the Company, its subsidiaries and associates for acknowledgement
and general practice as well as published such policy on the Company’s website.
The Company has implemented the aforementioned policies in various aspects to prevent and oppose corruption, summarized as
follows:
1. Guidelines have been provided on the control and prevention of any risks that may occur by determining the principles and
regulations on procurement, reimbursement, budget approval, charitable donations, entertainment expenditures or gift giving. In
addition, the guidelines specify that there shall be an internal control system in finance, accounting, data recording as well as other
internal processes related to accounting and finance to ensure confidence that the financial transactions are consistent with the
approved objectives and that the business operations are transparent etc.
2. The Company has communicated the policy on anti-corruption to various departments within the Company, the subsidiaries and
the associated companies through various channels; namely, employee training, intranet and the Company’s website for
acknowledgement and practice of related persons.
3. The Audit Committee has performed its duties in reviewing and ensuring that the Company has adequate good corporate governance,
in line with the anti-corruption policy and relevant laws.
4. The Company has provided channels to report any violations, unlawful acts, ethical misconduct or fraudulent or corrupt behavior
(Whistleblowing). In addition, the Company has set up the measure to protect  the whistleblower or complainant or any persons
cooperating in the reporting of such acts.

4) Information Disclosure and Transparency
The Company discloses financial statements, important information and other relevant information with potential impact on the
interests of the shareholders or investment decision which in turn may affect the price of the Company’s shares or securities in a
sufficient, timely and appropriate channels. The main purpose is to ensure that the decision to invest in securities of the Company
is made fairly and with equal information in accordance with the corporate information disclosure policy, the supervision of using
inside information policy and the securities trading policy to the directors, the management and employees for acknowledgement
and further action.
Furthermore, the Board of Directors has prepared the report on responsibility for the financial statement along with the report from
the auditors and also included these reports in the Company’s 56-1 One Report so that the management may recognize the
commitment and responsibility for information and financial report which must be accurate before being made available to the
investors.
Channels for Information Disclosure
Apart from information disclosure as mandated by law and regulations of related agencies, the Company has established the
Investor Relations unit as a channel for direct contact with both Thai and foreign investors and analysts, including any related persons
or external parties who are interested in the Company’s information. In communicating to external parties, the senior executives
who have been assigned the task of disclosing information about the Company are the President, the Chief Financial Officer and the
Assistant Vice President of Investor Relations as well as other related executives in accordance with the policy on information
disclosure.
For the year 2025, the senior executives of the Company and the Assistant Vice President of the Investor Relations provided
information to relevant parties in various occasions in both physical and online formats. Details are as follows:
1. Roadshows: 20 times, consisting of 1 roadshow abroad to meet foreign investors and 5 domestic roadshows. Online meetings
with a total of 15 times to facilitate the opportunity to meet more investors per each meeting, including a more variety of investors.
2. Analyst meetings: 8 times in total
3. Virtual meetings and conference calls, including questions and answers via e-mail: 327 times in total
4. Site visits for investors, analysts and other relevant parties for a total of 16 times, among which 1 time was held in Phuket.

9.1 Internal Control
The Company recognizes the importance of an internal
control system as a mechanism for prevention and manage-
ment of risks potentially arising to the Company to ensure
the Company’s achievement of the established objectives
in a sustainably effective and efficient manner. To achieve
this end, the Board of Directors has assigned the Audit
Committee to perform their respective duty independently
in supervising an adequate and suitable internal control
system. This includes monitoring the business operations
to prevent any conflicts of interest, considering related
party transactions and overseeing the Company’s operations
to safeguard its assets against fraud, damage and the leakage
of crucial information. Therefore, the Company has con-
sistently prepared accurate and credible accounting and
financial reports while simultaneously disclosing information
in a complete, sufficient and timely manner as well as
ensure the compliance with relevant rules, regulations and
laws relating to the Company’s business operations.
To achieve this end, the Company has established an
internal audit department which reports directly to the
Audit Committee. The Audit Committed has appointed Ms.

5) Monitoring Activities
The Company has monitored the compliance with the business ethics and the specifications which prohibit the management
and employees from acting in a way that may incur conflict of interest. The Company has constantly monitored and evaluated
internal control through assessments conducted by supervisors of each division, who possess adequate knowledge and ability,
and through internal audit reports whereby the results will be directly reported to the Audit Committee. The internal audit
department is responsible for supporting the Company’s operations to achieve its objectives by providing independent and
objective assurance and advisory services, supporting the operation of the outsourcing internal audit, and monitoring and
reporting progress of any rectifications and improvements of the inspected units for the Audit Committee’s acknowledgement
on a regular basis. Moreover, the internal audit department has supported the Audit Committee to perform their duty as
assigned by the Board of Directors and acts as the advisor in providing suggestions such as internal control, risk management,
information system security to other departments. The internal audit charter has been established to clearly determine
the purpose, scope of work, authority and responsibility of the internal audit function and has been reviewed on a regular
basis.
Audit Committee's Opinion
The opinion of the Board of Directors aligned with the Audit Committee (Details of the Audit Committee’s report
can be seen in Attachment 6). In addition, EY Office Company Limited, the Company’s auditor, has no additional opinions
regarding the Company’s 2025 internal control system.
9.2 Related Party Transactions
In cases where the Company and its subsidiaries enter into transactions with related parties, such actions will be
conducted in compliance with the Securities and Exchange Act, regulations or requirements of the Stock Exchange, and/
or announcements of the Capital Market Supervisory Board. Furthermore, to protect investors and ensure transparency,
if it is necessary for the Company and/or its subsidiaries to engage in transactions with related parties, the Company has
assigned the Audit Committee to oversee that these related party transactions are fairly conducted. In cases where the
Audit Committee lacks the expertise to review potential related party transactions, the Company has a policy to appoint
independent professionals with relevant expertise to provide opinions on such transactions which will be subsequently
informed the Audit Committee, and/or the Board of Directors, and/or the shareholders' meeting for making-decision, as
the case may be.

2) Financial Assistance

9.2.2 Trend of Future Connected Transactions
Type of Transactions Trend and Policy
Normal business transactions Normal business transactions
or transactions that support In the case that the Company and/or subsidiaries provide(s) medical services and
general business /or patient transfers, the Company has the policy for the Company and subsidiaries
to charge fees for such transactions between the Company and/or subsidiaries and
connected persons at the market rate. The rate charged, payment conditions and
other business conditions shall be equal to or similar to such rates and conditions
that the Company and/or subsidiaries charges and stipulates for transactions with
other persons or counterparties under the same situations.
Transactions that support general business
In the case that the Company and subsidiaries purchase goods and services, raw
materials and medical supplies, or procurements related to and in support of
medical services provided among the companies, or between the Company and
/or subsidiaries and connected persons, which are transactions in support of the
Company’s and/or subsidiary’s normal business, the product price or service fee
of such transactions must be at a referenced market rate. In addition, the payment
terms and trade agreements must not differ from the terms and conditions in which
the Company and/or subsidiary enters with other counter parties under the same
circumstances.
The Securities and Stock Exchange Act (Fourth Edition) Amendment B.E. 2551
effective from 31 August 2008 and the Securities and Exchange Commission circular
dated 16 July 2008 state that for flexibility, the Company may bring transactions
with associated companies which are normal business transactions or transactions
supporting normal business between the Company and subsidiaries and a related
person to the Board of Directors for approval in principle.
Therefore, at the Board of Directors Meeting 8/2008 on 27 August 2008, the Board
gave the management approval in principle to consider entering into normal busi-
ness or transaction supporting normal business transactions with normal trade
conditions, which are priced or have been charged at the market rate with the same
conditions as the Company’s transactions with external parties.

Financial Assistance The Company has the policy to provide financial assistance only to subsidiaries
in which the Company holds more than 50 percent of the outstanding shares.
The financial assistance is provided under the condition that the subsidiary has
no connected person that holds more than 10 percent of the remaining shares.
Such financial assistance will centralize financial management to manage costs
appropriately and efficiently for the highest benefits of the organization as a whole.
The Company has the policy to enter into 3 types of financial assistance transac-
tions as follows:
1. Loans to subsidiaries: The Company will take out loan in the money market
or from a financial institution and then loan that money to the subsidiary at the
rate of MLR-2.25 % per annum, which is lower than the rate the subsidiary would
be charged if taking out loan directly.
Benefit derived: The subsidiary can take out a loan at a rate lower than borrowing
directly from a financial institution as the Company is capable of seeking loans at
a lower rate.
2. Guarantee loans of foreign subsidiaries:
Benefit derived: To enable the foreign subsidiary to seek a loan directly from a
financial institution in that particular country at a prime rate.
3. Centralize Liquidity Management (Cash Pooling): At the end of the business
day, the liquidity management program will transfer money from the subsidiary’s
bank account to the Company’s bank account. The money transferred into the
Company’s bank account will be stated as short-term loan from a subsidiary with
interest charged at the market rate. At the same time, if any subsidiaries require
capital, the Company will transfer the excess liquidity to the subsidiary in need of
capital. Such money transferred will be stated as short-term loan to the subsidiary
with interest charged at the market rate as well.
Benefit derived: For liquidity management within the Group, the companies with
excess liquidity will receive interest at a rate higher than the savings rate of financial
institutions, while the companies in need of capital can borrow at a rate no higher
than the market rate.
In the case that it deemed necessary for the Company and/or subsidiaries to enter
